Until then an app's database keeps its schedules, and the embedder, in the server token's place, runs one on demand by `call`.

### Watching schedules

- *The listings*: `statements` gains `schedule` (`every 1h`, `at '0 3 * * *' in 'Europe/Berlin'`), `next` (the next slot's instant), `last` (the last mark, pending or landed: its slot, `ran` or `refused`, the status, the reason and `skipped`; `null` before the first) and `held` (a due slot the state holds back: its slot, the status and the first refusal's time; `null` otherwise); `describe statement` the same; `GET /_schema` lists them to the server's token, `?as=fennecql` writing them after the collections. A scoped token sees none.
- *The log*: a run past `--slow-ms` writes the slow line, `scheduled: ["purge", [h, c], slot, rows]`, its time the run's under the lock and never its lateness; a refused run writes a line naming what refused it, a held slot one as it is held and one as it is superseded, and a mark ahead of the clock the audit line `audit::clock`. Each carries an id of the run's own (`schedule::run_id`), as a request's lines carry its id ([http.md](http.md#request-ids)).
- *Traces*: a run is a trace of its own, begun and ended on the scheduler's thread: its root span's `db.operation.name` `schedule`, `db.stored_procedure.name` the name, `fennec.slot`, `fennec.lateness_ms` and `fennec.slots_skipped`, its statements' and bindings' spans beneath it as a call's; it never waits on the collector.
- *`/_stats/statements`* counts each as a shape of its own, `schedule purge`, its count the runs, its rows theirs, its time theirs under the lock, read as every shape is ([http.md](http.md#statement-counts)).
- *`/_metrics`*: `fennec_scheduled_runs_total{outcome}` (`ran`, `refused`, `broken`, `held`, `unknown`), `fennec_scheduled_skipped_total`, `fennec_scheduled_mark_ahead_total`, and `fennec_scheduled_lateness_seconds`, a histogram of a run's start past its slot; no label names a tenant, a statement or a collection ([http.md](http.md#metrics)). `monitoring/datadog/` gains monitors on refused and held runs, a mark ahead of the clock and lateness, held by `tests/datadog.rs`.
- *The studio* lists a database's schedules in the schema view with `next`, `last` and `held`, opens each as `create or replace statement`, and runs one now for the server's token; its highlighter learns `every`, `slot` and the `at` before a cron text (`make studio-highlight`).
- *The SDKs* send nothing new -- a definition is a text, and so is the listing -- and each reads `statements` into a type of its own, the four columns among the rest (slice 6: no SDK typed the listing before, so each gained it); `fennec types` writes nothing for a statement no one calls.

### What a schedule costs

**A database holding no schedule pays nothing a write, and its server nothing a minute.** The scheduler is beside the write path, never on it: no write asks it anything, and no collection gains a byte. A process holding no scheduled statement starts no scheduler until a definition, an open or an image adopted brings one (`schedule::watch`, woken where `Database::rebind` is, never by a timer), so an idle server wakes no more than it does now. Held by `a_server_holding_no_schedule_makes_no_look` -- a count of looks (`schedule::looks`) staying 0 over writes of every form -- and measured in turns against main as F2's slices were: in process and through `fennec_http::handle`, a `put`, an `insert`, a `set` and a `del` by id, a `put` of 1 000 documents and a `set` over a scan, each the median of nine rounds over five pairs, a pair run under someone else's load (`uptime`) discarded; `make requests-bench`; `make size-report` for the module and `fennec-server`. A path holding no schedule measures as it did, or the slice is not done.

**What a schedule costs is held to what it does.** A run finding nothing -- a purge of an empty range -- holds the write lock for its due check and its body's index probe, and lands nothing: no fsync, no record to an archive, no send to a standby, its mark riding the next write. Measured: its time under the lock, the bytes a pending mark adds to the write that carries it, and the fsyncs, archive records and sends of ten thousand tenants' empty runs at 03:00, counted and 0. At the floor a statement lands at most 1 440 marks a day, each dead as the next lands and given back by a compact (`CompactPolicy`), measured over a day's file. Beside them, `make requests-bench` with sixteen statements running every minute, its writes' p99 inside main's spread. On a tenant node, ten thousand tenants each with a statement due at 03:00: the last one's lateness, the median and p99, and the tenants opened a second, against a minute -- past it, the pool of schedulers.

### Scheduled statements against other systems

| System | Where and when it runs | As whom | fennecdb takes | fennecdb leaves |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| pg_cron | a background worker that "does not run any jobs as long as a server is in hot standby mode, but it automatically starts when the server is promoted"; five fields, `[1-59] seconds` and `$`; GMT unless `cron.timezone` says otherwise; "only one instance of each specific job at a time", a second queued ([pg_cron](https://github.com/citusdata/pg_cron)) | "the same permissions as the current user" who scheduled it | the primary alone, from its promotion; cron's five fields; one run of a job at a time | a job's command as text, unchecked against the schema; its history in `cron.job_run_details`, "not cleaned automatically", recorded beside the job's transaction rather than in it; seconds; no catch-up of slots missed while down |
| Supabase Cron | pg_cron, running "SQL snippets or database functions ... or make an HTTP request", "from every second to once a year", "no more than 8 Jobs run concurrently" advised ([Supabase Cron](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/cron)) | pg_cron's | each job listed with its runs | HTTP calls from the database; seconds |
| Cloud Scheduler, Firebase `onSchedule` | a managed service calling a function at a unix-cron time in a zone, "at least once" and in rare cases "multiple times" for one scheduled time ([Cloud Scheduler](https://cloud.google.com/scheduler/docs/overview)); "a function may be triggered multiple times, with the next instance running while the previous instance is still executing" ([scheduled functions](https://firebase.google.com/docs/functions/schedule-functions)) | a service account | a zone a schedule; the slot handed to the run (`scheduleTime`: `slot()`) | delivery at least once, idempotency left to the function; overlapping runs; a service outside the data |
| PocketBase | `cronAdd(name, expr, fn)`, JavaScript run "in its own goroutine as part of the `serve` command process", "previewed and triggered from the _Dashboard > Settings > Crons_ section" ([jobs scheduling](https://pocketbase.io/docs/js-jobs-scheduling/)) | the app's | a job by name, listed, run by hand from the dashboard | a JavaScript engine; a job in code beside the data, not in it; a run in no transaction with its mark |
| SQLite | none: the app's own timer, or the system's `cron`, runs a statement | the connection's | nothing to take: the native library runs none either | -- |

### F3 in slices

Each a pull request of its own, green and measured, carrying its own docs, as F2's were, and each fails closed: a slice never runs anything whose rules a later slice owes. Slices 1 and 2 refuse a scheduled definition outside a test. Slice 3, the first that lets one be defined, carries every rule of who may define, call, list and see one and every refusal's class, mark and line, so no schedule runs or is called a day before its authority holds; a tenant node refuses a definition until slice 4 brings its runs there. A schedule defined where nothing runs it is a slot no one runs without a word, the one thing F3 must never do; one run before its rules is the elevation F1's definer model keeps out.

1. **The parser and the tree** (fennec-ql, `fennec_core::named`). `every <interval>`, `at '<cron>'` and `in '<zone>'` in a definition, the cron text read into its five bitmasks; `slot()` in a scheduled body alone; the refusals the text alone answers ([above](#the-form-of-a-schedule)), `after` beside a schedule among them; the schedule in the canonical tree, written only where it holds one. Execution refuses a scheduled definition in words of its own ("scheduled statements are not built yet"), the module in its own. Owes: `make fennecql-corpus BASE=origin/main`, no tree changed and no stored digest moved; fennec-cli's `kind`, a mark in `Parser::cut` where `in '<zone>'` may follow the schedule at the text's end, and the shell corpus' lines; the parse time of texts that are no definition, as F1's slice 1 measured it.
2. **The record and the builds that run none** (fennec-core) -- **built** ([below](#what-f3-has-built)). Op 4 and op 5, `since`, `writes_in`, an image's last mark, a compact's, `Undo::Mark` and `Database::note`, a replica's apply and `changes_in`; a replacement clearing the mark; a pending mark (`Database::pending`) landing as the last record of the next block `commit` lands, kept by a block put back, and written by an image; `grows` passing over op 5; `depends`, and the kind kept across a replacement; `MAX_SCHEDULED`; `Database::due`, a pure function of the table, the marks and a time, held by the clock and summer-time tests below; `tests/statements.fennec` gaining a schedule and a mark (`FENNEC_WRITE_FIXTURE=1`), which the module opens and keeps through its images. Definitions run where a test lets them, as F2's slice 2 had them. Owes: the module's bytes (`make size-report BASE=origin/main`, all three builds).
3. **Running them on a file's server** (fennec-http's `schedule`, fennec-server). The scheduler thread, started after both listeners, asleep with no schedule, `stop_with` where it cannot start; a run as the server's token's call, in a block with its mark, its slot asked again under the lock and made durable after it; `now()` and `slot()`; once at the latest and `skipped`; a run landing nothing marked in memory, the watcher told `Some(&[])`; the refusal classes -- the statement's marked, the state's held (`schedule::Held`) and tried again as the counter or the limit moves or the backoff ends, the next slot superseding it -- the fence's refusal told from a hook's; the clock's rule, `AHEAD_MAX` and `audit::clock`; a following database, `--http-read-only` and a storage failure passed over; `schedule::pass(db, now)` taking the time, so a test drives a slot without waiting for one. With them every rule of [a run's authority](#a-runs-authority): a definition by the server's token alone, logged by its shape and naming the schedule; `call` of one by the server's token, its `slot()` truncated to the schedule's slot and its run marking nothing; a scoped token's call, at `/query`, `/batch` and `POST /_call/<name>`, the 404 a missing statement's is; a `call` line naming one logged and granting nothing; no MCP tool; a scheduled statement held out of every listing and schema answer a scoped token reads; the refused and held runs' log lines with their ids (`schedule::run_id`); the sync's scenario, `NAMED` refusing the definition already. Definitions run for the server's token on a file's server, and are refused on a tenant node until slice 4, and so is the install of an image holding one, a tenant opening with one said to hold it unrun -- never a schedule no one runs without a word. The FennecQL reference's section; `make upgrade-check`'s base before op 4, and RELEASING.md's and the runbook's notes, since a primary can write op 4 from here; CLAUDE.md's rule ([below](#what-claudemd-would-gain-from-f3)). Owes: [the measurements above](#what-a-schedule-costs), every path holding no schedule as on main; `make sync-scenarios-check`.
4. **Tenant nodes** (fennec-http's `tenants`, fennec-shard) -- **built** ([below](#what-f3-has-built)). Slice 3's refusals lifted -- a definition, and an image holding one at an install or a move. The tenant's gate and a frozen tenant passed over, the lease's fence and the lapse after a run's landing counted; the hint naming the scheduled tenants alone, entered before a first schedule lands and by every way a file comes on, holding closed tenants' pending marks; an idle tenant opened when due, its run no use of it (`last_used` untouched) and the tenant closed after it; the queue by name, never an `Arc`; the round robin, the quota as a run lands and no rate; a failover, a move and a move undone, each a test in `tests/lease_nodes.rs`' shape; the router reading a schema declaring a schedule with the grammar. The sharding pages' section. Owes: the lateness at ten thousand tenants; `make shard-bench ARGS=latency` inside its spread.
5. **The surfaces left** (fennec-http, fennec-abi) -- **built** ([below](#what-f3-has-built)), each for the server's token, none an authority or a refusal: the listings' `schedule`, `next`, `last` and `held` and `/_schema`'s, the slow line, the spans, `/_stats/statements`' shape, `/_metrics`' four; the Datadog monitors. README's feature table, the home page and docs index where a visitor picks a database for work on a schedule, the monitoring and sync pages, and the search cache. Owes: `tests/datadog.rs` holding the monitors to the scrapes.
6. **The studio, the SDKs and the shell** -- **built** ([below](#what-f3-has-built)). The studio's schedules in its schema view, "run now" and the highlighter; each SDK's `statements` row and the SDK lists; the shell's `.help`. Owes: `make studio-test` and each SDK's suite.

**The tests the design asks for**, each failing with its part taken out, beside each slice's own:

- across a failover: a lease node runs a slot and loses its lease before its stream sends the block, the stream held; the standby promoted runs that slot once, its `slot()` the same; with the block sent, the promoted copy runs nothing for it; the old node, fenced past its lease, lands no run;
- across a move: a frozen source runs nothing after its export and the target runs the slot due once; a move undone leaves the source to run it;
- a crash between a run's landing and its fsync, the tail cut on open, runs the slot once on reopen; one after the fsync runs nothing;
- a run landing nothing: no fsync, no archive record, no send, counted; its mark landing as the last record of the next write's block, and with the checkpoint's image; a crash before either runs the slot again once, over the data the lost run read; a tenant's pending mark kept by the hint across its close and dropped once its counter moved; a held `claim` not woken by it (`Some(&[])`);
- the clock: stepped back staying past the mark, nothing runs again; stepped back behind the mark by under `AHEAD_MAX`, a pause and nothing run twice, by more, the schedule going on from the clock, each counted with its audit line; stepped forward three periods, one run, `skipped` 2; a definition's `since` running no slot from before it, a replacement's moving it and clearing a mark a year ahead;
- each row of the refusals' table: the statement's refused and marked with its status and reason, the next slot run; a panic marked once; a broken statement's slots marked as they pass, the next run once it reads again; the state's held unmarked -- a run at the quota tried again once a delete moves the counter or a raised quota the limit, at most once a minute, and after the backoff with neither, superseded by the next slot with `skipped` 1; a lapsed lease, a frozen tenant, a following database and a storage failure unmarked and due; a lease lapsing mid-run leaving the slot due where a mirrored collection's guard marks it;
- each place running none: a replica, a read replica, a following tenant, `--http-read-only`, the shell, the native library, the module; a promoted replica running from its promotion;
- the one shutdown: a signal while a run holds the lock, the run landed whole and synced or not at all; a server failing at its port having written no run;
- a run waking a binding, both in one block; a held `claim` answered by a run's write; a late run reading `alive` by its start;
- summer time, through `Database::due` and a server: `at '30 2 * * *' in 'Europe/Berlin'` the night the clocks go forward runs at the jump, once, and the night they go back once; `every 1h` in that zone twice in the repeated hour, its slots held to `bucket`'s edges;
- the grammar's refusals, Friday the 13th and 30 February among them;
- a tenant node: a closed tenant opened when its slot comes, its `last_used` untouched and the tenant closed after its run, a hot tenant never evicted for it; a start opening no tenant that holds no schedule; the hint removed and relearned; a tenant entering the hint before its first schedule lands, a crash between leaving it named; a tenant installed, restored or moved in learned; a thousand tenants due at once, each run once before any twice;
- the quota: a setting run at the quota refused 507 and held, its slot run once room comes; a purge let through, and a client's purge carrying a pending mark;
- an F2 binary stopping at op 4 and op 5 in words (`make upgrade-check`);
- `call` of a scheduled statement, from slice 3: the 404 to a scoped token, run unmarked for the server's token, its `slot()` the latest slot at or before its `now()`, no MCP tool, a `call` line logged, absent from a scoped token's listings;
- `a_server_holding_no_schedule_makes_no_look`.

### What F3 has built

**Slice 1, the parser and the tree.** *What it lets a user do: nothing yet, by design* -- every definition is refused where statements run, so no schedule is stored that no one runs.

*The grammar.* `create [or replace] statement <name> (every <interval> | at '<cron>') [in '<zone>'] [comment '<text>'] [scans] as <body>` reads into `Definition::schedule`, a `Schedule` boxed as a binding is, so a callable definition carries a pointer for it: `When::Every`, `bucket`'s `query::Interval` -- a step of milliseconds from the epoch or a week's Monday, or calendar months -- read by `named::period` from a number and its unit as `bucket(at, 15m)` reads one (`every 1 d` too, the unit in any case); or `When::At`, a `Cron` of five masks, minutes in 60 bits, hours 24, days of the month 31, months 12 and days of the week 7, Sunday's 0 and 7 one bit, read by `Cron::read` from `*`, numbers, ranges, lists, steps and the three-letter English names in any case; then the zone `tz::zone_of` reads, written by `tz::spelled`: `None` for UTC however written -- `'UTC'`, `'utc'`, `'Z'`, `'+00:00'` are the clock no `in` reads, as [the form](#the-form-of-a-schedule) has it -- another fixed offset as `+hh:mm`, and a zone of the table by the table's own spelling of its name. `comment` and `scans` follow in either order, `in` right after the schedule. The cron text and the zone are quoted texts, so `lexer::Unclosed` learns nothing.

*The refusals the text alone answers*, each at the word it is about and naming the form to write: an interval under a minute or not whole minutes (`every 30s`, `every 90s`; `every 120s` is `every 2m`), `named::MIN_PERIOD`; an interval with no unit or one `bucket` does not take (`every 1 hour`, `every 1.5h`); a cron expression of other than five fields, six named as Quartz's seconds and `every 1m`; a value outside its field, a step of 0 or past the field's highest value (`*/90` names `every`), a range running backwards (`fri-sun`, naming Sunday 7 at a range's end), a step after a lone value (`5/15`, naming `5-59/15`); Quartz's `?`, `L`, `W` and `#` and pg_cron's `$`; each macro named as what it is (`@hourly` `every 1h`, `@daily` and `@midnight` `every 1d`, `@weekly` `at '0 0 * * 0'`, `@monthly` `every 1mo`, `@yearly` `every 1y`, `@reboot` no time at all); a day of the month and a day of the week both written as other than `*` -- `0 0 13 * 5`, naming `at '0 0 13 * *'` and `at '0 0 * * 5'` for either and a guard for both, and `0 0 1-31 * mon` and `0 0 13 * 0-6` as well, which name every day in one field and run every day to cron, and `0 0 */1 * mon`, since only `*` is open to every reader (the review found the masks read here, which ran the first on Mondays and the second on the 13th: [the form](#the-form-of-a-schedule)); an expression that never runs, its lowest day held to the longest month named (`0 0 30 2 *`, `0 0 31 apr,jun,sep,nov *`; 29 February runs). A zone the binary's table lacks (`tz::zone_of`, as `bucket` reads one) is refused by the parser, not by the engine as this section first had it: the table is the binary's, so the text alone answers, and a text a later binary wrote naming a zone this one lacks reads no tree here and is marked broken -- a clock no run of this build could read. Parameters and their parentheses, `claims`, `check` and `public` (`named::SCHEDULED_VALUES`), `$1`, `$a` and `$jwt.sub` in the body, and `new.` and `old.` in any case, as a value or a key; `after` beside a schedule, either order (`named::SCHEDULED_AND_BOUND`), and a second schedule. In the body, beside F1's refusals, a `get` without `require`, `returning`, and a body of guards alone (`Definition::scheduled_refused`, out of line), a tree made by hand held there to the same.

*`slot()`* is `Expr::Call("slot", [])`, read in a scheduled body alone: `call`'s match on the function's name, which every call passes already, holds one arm more, `"slot"` where a definition is being read, and that arm (`Parser::slot`, `#[cold]`) refuses it in a callable body or `check` and in a binding's body or condition, in any case, and `slot(1)`. Out of a definition `slot()` is the call it is on main, which the engine finds no function for.

*The canonical tree.* The schedule is tag 10 of the definition, after the binding's 9, a node of kind 6 -- the step (1), its origin (2, a week's Monday), the months (3), or the five masks (4 to 8), then the zone (9) -- written only where a definition holds one, and `Debug` writes `schedule` only there too: every other definition's bytes are as they were, `GRAMMAR_EPOCH` stays 2, and the reaper's digest is pinned (`a_schedule_is_in_the_canonical_tree`). It holds the meaning, never the spelling: `every 60m` is `every 1h`, `24h` is `1d`, `12mo` is `1y`, `'*/15 * * * *'` is `'0,15,30,45 * * * *'` and Sunday 7 is 0, while `1w` is not `7d` -- a week starts on a Monday. The zone is the one `tz::zone_of` reads, never its spelling (`tz::spelled`, as the review found the text written): `in 'UTC'`, `'utc'`, `'Z'`, `'+00:00'` and `'-00'` write no zone, as no `in` does, UTC being the clock a schedule with none reads; `'+0300'` and `'+03'` are `'+03:00'`; and `'europe/berlin'` is `Europe/Berlin`, the table's own spelling, never the table's entry number, which a table built from another tzdata numbers otherwise. A link is a name of its own -- `'Asia/Calcutta'` is not `'Asia/Kolkata'`, nor `'Etc/UTC'` UTC's offset, a named zone refusing a time before 2000 -- since which name a link leads to moves with tzdata (`Europe/Kiev` became `Europe/Kyiv`'s in 2022b) and a digest must not move with a binary's table. No stored definition holds a schedule yet, so no digest moves and `GRAMMAR_EPOCH` stays 2. `make fennecql-corpus BASE=origin/main`, run again after the review: 10 570 texts, 21 140 parses, 21 054 read alike and no tree changed; 16 parse that were refused, each a schedule -- the tests', the shell's, the design's two and the module test's; 4 that parsed are refused, each a test of this branch's showing `slot()` refused outside a scheduled body (a callable body, a `check`, a binding's body and its condition), which main reads as a call of a function the engine has none of; and 23 are refused in other words: F1's `create statement s() every 1h` and *What it does not do*'s `purge() public every 1h`, now refused at their parentheses as a schedule's, the module test's `at '0 0 30 2 *'`, now refused as never running, and the scheduled tests' refusals and templates.

*Nothing runs one.* `Database::define_statement` refuses a definition holding a schedule before it asks anything else (`named::SCHEDULED_NOT_BUILT`, "scheduled statements are not built yet", a query error, 400) -- `/query`, `/batch`, whose block is put back, the shell, the native library and a declared schema's apply all define through it (`a_schedule_is_refused_where_statements_run`, fennec-http's `a_schedule_is_refused_until_schedules_run`); a scoped token meets the schema change it is (403). The module refuses it as every definition, `UNBUILT`, its parser built without named statements. A definition's record whose text reads as a schedule, digest and all, is none this build writes -- op 4 is slice 2's -- and `read_statements` marks it broken (`its text reads as a scheduled statement`), never loading it as callable (`a_schedule_s_text_in_a_callable_record_is_marked_broken`).

*The shell.* No arm of fennec-cli's `kind` is new: a scheduled definition is `CreateStatement`, a write with one body statement and a schema change with `begin ... end`, as every definition. A mark in `Parser::cut` is new, but not where the plan put it: `in '<zone>'` after the schedule needs none, the form never parsing before `as` and its body, while `every 1` at the text's end -- its unit on the next line -- was refused there and then, which `a_statement_typed_up_to_a_word_is_left_open` found; the mark makes it "go on". Four lines join `a_statement_written_over_lines_reads_as_one`'s corpus, and `a_schedule_over_lines_reads_as_one_statement` pipes one, its unit and zone on lines of their own, through the shell to the engine's refusal.

*Size and time.* The browser module against main (`make size-report BASE=origin/main`): raw and code the same to the byte with every feature, without the indexes and with none; gzip +1, +4 and +1, brotli +79, +192 and +112, each the report's noise -- the cron reader, `period` and the canonical writer's schedule are reached only from fennec-ql's `statements` path, which the module is built without. What a text that is no definition pays is that arm of `call`'s match, and `create statement`'s look for `every` and `at` once a definition; its parse time, the corpus's 10 136 texts that are no definition parsed by main's build and this branch's in turns, 11 pairs at a 1-minute load under 1.5: the median pair -0.17% (1 177.0 ns a text on main, 1 175.0 here), every pair between -0.46% and +0.41% -- no cost. It was measured before the review's fixes, which touch the definition's path alone and `tz`'s lookup by name, the same work. `fennec-server`'s code against main (`make size-report BIN=fennec-server`, this branch and its base on main each, after the review): 4 210.9 KB against 4 194.8, +16.1 KB (+0.38%), fennec-ql's +9.1 and fennec-core's +6.5 -- the cron reader, `period`, the schedule's refusals and canonical writer and `tz::spelled`, every one reached from a definition alone, out of line: what a server pays for the grammar it will run.

**Slice 2, the record and the builds that run none.** *What it lets a user do: nothing yet, by design* -- as F2's slice 2 did, the record lands while every way in still refuses a definition (`named::SCHEDULED_NOT_BUILT`), `Database::allow_schedules` (`#[doc(hidden)]`) letting a test define one: no server writes op 4 before one runs it, and the upgrade's order is held by a fixture rather than owed by a primary.

*The records.* Op 4 is `[13][0][length][4][history][change][epoch][digest][name][depends][text][schedule][since]`, the schedule `[1][step][origin]` for an `every` of minutes, hours, days or weeks (`bucket`'s `Interval::Ms`, a week's origin its Monday), `[2][months]` for `mo` and `y`, or `[3]` and cron's five masks, each a varint; then the zone's name as the tree writes it (`tz::spelled`), empty for UTC, and `since`, epoch milliseconds as a zigzag varint, as a timestamp is written. Op 5 is `[13][0][length][5][name][slot][history][change][outcome][status][skipped][reason]`: the slot as a zigzag varint, the version of the definition that ran, `1` ran -- its status 0 and its reason empty -- or `2` refused, with a status of 100 to 599 and the reason, and the slots it passed over. In a server's memory they are `Stored::schedule` (`Timing`: `Times` -- `Step`, `Months` or `Cron` -- the zone and `since`) and the marks beside the table (`SlotMark`, *The mark* below). Refused there as `a statement record this version does not read`, never read otherwise: an op past 5, a schedule's tag past 3, a step not of whole minutes or an origin past it, months outside 1 to 1 200, a cron mask empty or past its field, an outcome past 2, a ran mark with a status or a reason, a refused one with a status no answer has, a byte past the schedule or the reason -- what a later version's finer schedule or another outcome would be (`a_schedule_or_a_mark_this_version_does_not_read_is_refused`). The schedule comes after the text, the record's tail: before the text, as first built, a build that runs none had to walk it to find the text.

*The module keeps both as bytes.* A build without the `statements` feature -- the browser module -- runs no schedule, so it reads of them only what it must to keep them: an op 4's tail after the text is its `Timing`, the bytes as they came, never read, and an op 5 is read for its name and version alone -- the slot between them passed over -- and kept whole as its statement's last mark (`Stored::last`) where it names the definition the table holds, an image writing it back after its definition as it came. So the module no longer refuses a schedule or an outcome a later version writes, which it did while it read them whole. That is safe because nothing in it acts on either: it runs no slot, counts none and answers no `due`, so there is no reading of the bytes to get wrong, and the bytes it writes back are the ones it took -- the server that next opens the file reads them and refuses what it does not read, as it would have refused the file the page opened. What the module does read it still holds to: a mark whose name, slot or version is cut short, or an op 4 with no tail, is refused, and a mark naming another definition -- one replaced or dropped since -- is passed over, as a server passes it over (`web/fennec.test.js` keeps a later slot's mark of outcome 9 as it came, in place of the fixture's).

*One write each.* `writes_in` counts op 4 and op 5 as it counts every kind-13 record, a mark noted as a schema change of no collection is (`SCHEMA_MARK` on collection 0), so a replica's counter stays its primary's and a mark is sent as any write. A definition's `since` is the clock it is defined by (`Database::now`, a test's `set_clock`); a replacement moves it and clears the mark, its new `Stored` holding none. The kind is the record's: `named::Kind` -- `Called`, `Bound` or `Scheduled` -- is what `kind_kept` compares, so a callable or bound statement replaced by a scheduled one, or the other way, is refused naming the `drop` and `create` it needs, while a schedule, its zone and its body change freely (`breaks_contract` asks a scheduled one nothing more). `MAX_SCHEDULED`, 16, is refused past it, a replacement taking no place. `depends` is its body's, as a callable statement's: a schema change breaking it is refused at its commit naming it.

*The mark.* A server keeps each scheduled statement's last landed mark beside the statements' table, not in it (`Database::marks`, read through `Database::last_mark`): the table is a cell readers hold past their lock, and a mark landing in it made a new one, every statement copied -- texts, dependencies and all -- a slot a tenant (the review's finding). A mark is read under the database's lock alone, so beside the table it changes its own entry and nothing else, never the table's `Arc`, which a reader comparing by `Arc::ptr_eq` would have taken for a definition changed every slot; one naming a definition replaced or dropped since marks nothing, and a replacement or a drop takes its name's. `Database::mark_slot(name, SlotMark)` is how slice 3's runs will mark a slot: where the open block holds writes -- the run's -- the mark is its last record, landing and put back with it (`Undo::Mark`, the mark before; a replacement's or a drop's taking of one goes back the same way); otherwise it waits in `Database::pending`, one word of the database's (`None` while none waits, so `commit` asks a word it holds and follows no pointer), until `commit` lands a block: there `carry_pending` appends each pending mark that counts as the block's last records, and `landed_pending` makes each its statement's last mark once the block landed, a block put back -- a lapsed lease's refusal, a quota's -- keeping them pending. A pending mark counts only where it names the definition the table holds (`pending_of`): one of a definition replaced is dropped by the next block that lands. It is later than the landed mark by order, never by slot: a run's mark landing in its block takes its name's pending one (`mark_slot`, given back by `Undo::Mark` where the block is put back), so a pending mark is always the newer. As first built it counted only past the landed mark's slot, and slice 3's review found the slot it ran again: a mark a wrong clock left more than `AHEAD_MAX` ahead, landed, and the clock put back, every run finding nothing ran the same slot again at each look, its pending mark outranked by the one ahead. A lone schema change outside a block carries none; `create collection` is a block, and carries them. An image writes each schedule's last mark right after its definition, a pending one in the landed one's place, and leaves it pending: the checkpoint the one shutdown writes holds it, and the next block still sends it to the standbys. `grows` passes over op 5, so a delete carrying a mark is let through at a quota, and so does `app_writes`: a request whose block carries a run's mark is not charged a write of its rate for it.

*Compacts.* A compact under the lock writes an image, so keeps each schedule's last mark alone. One beside the database (`Database::maintain`) writes the definitions into its side file as they stood and the marks at its swap, under the write lock, each schedule's last as it stands then; its check that nothing changed meanwhile asks the definitions alone (`Statements::defines_as`, the same names at the same versions), which a mark, kept beside the table, never changes: a schedule every minute fails no compact taking longer (`a_compact_beside_the_database_keeps_a_mark_landed_meanwhile`).

*Replicas and readers.* A replica's apply and an open read op 4 into the table and op 5 into the marks beside it, a mark of a definition replaced or dropped since passed over (`Statements::marks`); `changes_in` hands each over as `ChangeKind::Statement`, which `/_changes` passes over, its cursor with it; an image adopted brings its table and its marks, the pending marks dropped -- a following database marks none.

*`Database::due`* (`engine/due.rs`, the `statements` feature's): a pure function of the table, the marks and a time, giving each scheduled statement the slot to run -- the latest at or before the time and after both `since` and its last mark, pending or landed -- and the slots it passes over (`skipped`), the first slot after (`next`), and a mark or `since` ahead of the time (`ahead`). Ahead by at most `named::AHEAD_MAX`, an hour, its slots wait for the clock to pass it; further ahead, the reading is taken for the wrong one and anchors nothing, the schedule going on from the clock -- with neither a mark nor `since` left to anchor it, from the latest slot, once. A zone this binary's table lacks computes no slot. `at '<cron>'` and `every` of whole days, weeks and months name times of the clock, each run at the first instant the clock reads it or later: the furthest the clock read by an instant decides which have come, and the times a forward jump passes together are one slot. `every` shorter than a day runs at `bucket`'s edges: each instant the clock reads one, and each forward jump past one. Each is a walk over the zone's stretches of one offset (`Zone::stretch` and `Zone::first_reading`, added to `tz.rs`) and over the clock's times by arithmetic -- a cron expression's a day at a time, eight years at most for a 29 February -- so a server down for a year counts its missed slots without a walk over each. Held by `the_slots_are_the_long_way_s_across_every_change`: twelve schedules -- `every` 15m, 1h, 2h, 90m, 1d, 1w and 1mo and five cron expressions -- in UTC, `+05:45`, Berlin, Lord Howe (half an hour each way) and Santiago (its changes at midnight: a day starting twice, and one with no midnight), across each change of 2026, against the long way round a minute at a time, each minute's `bucket` and each cron time's first instant; and through `Database::due` by the summer-time nights the design names and the clock's rules (`tests/scheduled.rs`).

*Nothing runs one.* Every way in refuses a definition as slice 1 had it (`SCHEDULED_NOT_BUILT`, held by fennec-ql's and fennec-http's tests unchanged) but a test that called `allow_schedules`. A call of one is refused by `Database::callee`, "statement `x` runs by the clock: no call runs it", before anything of it is judged; to a scoped token it is the 404 a missing statement's is (`access.rs`, as a binding's), and the MCP server makes no `call_<name>` tool of it (`Stored::called`): slice 3 brings the server token's call and every rule of it. `statements` lists it, its `after` `null`; the listings' `schedule`, `next` and `last` are slice 5's. The browser module and the native library keep both records through their images and run none: the module refuses `call` as it refuses every call, and nothing in the native library asks `due`.

*The fixture.* `tests/statements.fennec` gains `lapse`, `every 15m in 'Europe/Berlin'` over a collection of its own (`holds`), defined at 12:00 UTC on 28 March 2026, and a run of its 13:00 slot, three passed over, marked in the block of its write (`FENNEC_WRITE_FIXTURE=1`, `scheduled::with_schedule`, written again from main's file when the schedule moved after the text): every build reads both, this one computes its next slot from them, and the module opens the file and keeps the text and the mark's bytes through its images (`web/fennec.test.js`).

*The upgrade.* `make upgrade-check` gains `OP4_BASE`, `cd907d6c`, the last main before op 4: its server refuses to open the fixture in words ("a statement record this version does not read"), an old replica of this tree's server serving it refuses the image and connects again, an old archiver keeps the image it is sent and takes none of its own once a write follows ("no image taken"), and its `fennec types` refuses the file; this tree's server opens it, answers its reads, takes its writes and refuses `call lapse`. Run whole, from the kind's base and op 3's, and again after the review, the fixture written again with the schedule after the text: every step passed. No primary defines one until slice 3, which brings RELEASING.md's note.

*Size.* The browser module against main (`make size-report BASE=origin/main`, after the review): with every feature +1 281 bytes raw, +537 gzip and +476 brotli, its code +1 265 -- `take_statement` +832, reading op 4's tail and op 5's name and version and keeping their bytes, `image_into` +224, writing each mark after its definition, and `Stored`'s clone +185 for its two fields; without the indexes (`fennec-lite`) +1 279, +644 and +507, and with no feature +1 279, +586 and +576. As first built it was +3 659 raw, +1 535 gzip and +1 219 brotli (`fennec-lite` +3 649, +1 567 and +1 420; no feature +3 633, +1 570 and +1 276), the review's blocking finding: the module read both records whole -- the schedule into `Times`, a zone and `since`, a mark's outcome, status, skips and reason -- and wrote each mark again from them (`marked`), `take_statement` +2 201, `image_into` +893 and the clone +379, for a module that runs no schedule. The review's cut, its prototype measured at +1 743 raw and +753 brotli: the module keeps a schedule as its bytes and a mark as its body, written back as it came, never `marked`. Then four more, each measured: the schedule after the text, so the module takes the record's tail rather than walking the schedule to find the text (0.2 KB of `take_statement`); a mark kept on its statement's entry (`Stored::last`) rather than in a table beside it as a server keeps one (*The mark*, above), whose walk, growth and drop were 0.4 KB; each mark written in the definitions' walk, right after its own, rather than in a second walk through a closure (0.15 KB); and a mark's name read as the definitions' are (`decode_str`) rather than borrowed (`text_at`), a copy of that reader the module otherwise lacks (0.2 KB). The records are core, as op 1's and op 3's are: the module keeps what a server's file holds through its images, the price of a page writing a server's file (`openFile`) without losing a schedule. The rest is the `statements` feature's, which the module is built without: `Database::due` and its slot math, the marks beside the table and the pending ones, `mark_slot` and every check of a schedule's values or a mark's outcome. Two records at 476 bytes brotli, against op 1's 824 and op 3's 495 -- brotli's noise is 0.3 KB, so as op 3's. The docs' figures for the module without the indexes and with none move with them (`javascript.html`, `benchmarks-size.html`, which `site/build.py` holds within 1%): 206 and 183 KB brotli, 246 and 218 gzip, 593 and 533 raw.

*Time.* A write to a database holding no schedule asks one word more of the database as its block lands (`pending`, `None`), and again once it landed; nothing else on the write path changed. Measured as F2's slices were -- `insert`, `set` by id and `del` by id in process, 50 000 each over a fresh database, main's build and this branch's in turns, seven pairs, each run the median of nine rounds, made once the 1-minute load is under 1.5 and made again where it rose past 1.8, none needing it: `insert` 364.4 ns on main and 358.2 here (the median pair -1.43%, every pair between -3.14% and +0.92%), `set` by id 573.0 and 569.9 (-0.56%, -2.60% to +0.93%), `del` by id 380.0 and 380.1 (-0.31%, -0.61% to +0.69%) -- no cost, so no count of instructions was owed.

**Slice 3, running them on a file's server.** *What it lets a user do:* define a scheduled statement on a `fennec-server <file>` primary with the server's token, by `/query`, `/batch` or a declared schema's apply, and have it run once a slot, its writes and its mark one block.

*The scheduler* (`fennec_http::schedule`). One thread a process, `fennec-schedule`, started by `schedule::watch` from fennec-server's main after both listeners are bound and once `Server::new` gave the database its `--call-max-rows` and its watcher, before the endpoint takes a request: a first look may run a missed slot, and a server failing at a port has written none (`a_server_failing_at_its_port_writes_no_run`). The plan had a database holding no schedule start no scheduler; built, the thread starts at the server's start and waits on a condition with no deadline while no database it watches holds a schedule. Started at the first definition, a thread that could not be made would fail inside that request, its definition landed with nothing to run it -- the slot no one runs F3 must never leave; started here, a failure is `stop_with`'s before the server serves (exit 2), and a parked thread costs its stack's few touched pages and no look: the engine wakes it where `rebind` finds a schedule in the table (`Database::on_schedules`, a hook a database holds, told the count of schedules under the write lock and answering with a flag and a notify), never a timer, so a server holding none looks at nothing (`a_server_holding_no_schedule_makes_no_look`, which counts the looks). With a schedule the thread sleeps until the earliest next slot, a minute at most, so a clock stepped forward is found within one. `watch` is also what lets a database take a definition (`Database::allow_schedules`): one no scheduler watches refuses it, `named::SCHEDULED_UNRUN` ("no scheduler runs this database's statements: a file's `fennec-server` runs them"), which replaces slice 1's `SCHEDULED_NOT_BUILT` -- the shell, the native library, a tenant node and a server under `--http-read-only` (`a_tenant_node_takes_no_scheduled_statement_yet`, `the_native_library_runs_no_schedule`, `a_schedule_is_refused_where_no_scheduler_runs_it`). Under `--http-read-only` no scheduler starts, and the server says how many it holds unrun (`a_read_only_server_runs_none_and_says_so`) -- and again, through the same hook, as a replica's apply brings it more (`schedule::unrun`, `a_read_only_replica_says_so_as_a_schedule_reaches_it`). The thread's loop is caught whole: a panic outside a run's own catch, or the loop's end, stops the server through `stop_with` (`a_panic_outside_a_run_stops_the_server`), where first built it ended the thread without a word and left the server serving with its schedules unrun.

*A look* (`schedule::pass(db, now)`, which a test drives with the time it names). Under the read lock it asks `Database::due` and whether this copy may write -- not following, no storage failure, the fence open (`Database::fenced`, made public) -- and tells a mark found ahead of the clock once for each mark: a log line and the audit log's `clock` event (`audit::clock`, its database, statement, mark, clock and `ahead_ms`, and whether its slots wait), counted. Each due slot then runs under the write lock, `due` asked again there, so a slot two looks found runs once (`a_run_and_its_mark_land_as_one_block_once`). `Database::run_slot(name, slot, now)` runs the body as the server's token's call does -- no scope, no claim, no rate, a fresh `--call-max-rows` -- its clock fixed to the run's start for the run -- read once the write lock is held, the later of the look's time and the clock, so the sixteenth run of a look, behind fifteen others' fsyncs and streams, reads the time it runs at (first built, every run of a look read the look's) -- so `now()` is the run's start and `@ttl`'s `alive` reads it too (`a_run_wakes_a_binding_in_its_block_and_reads_alive_by_its_start`), and `slot()` the slot: a thread-local `Registry::call` asks only where the function table holds no function of the name, so every other call pays nothing for it and a build without `statements` carries none of it. The body runs through the call's own write loop (`Database::run_callee`, a `Callee` whose `slot` is the run's), out of line where a slot is read (`run_slotted`); the clock and the slot are given back by drop guards, the scheduler's `catch_unwind` the one a run has. Each body statement's filter is handed `slot()` as written, so `where period = slot()` reaches an index as `where period = $1` does, and `body_checked` holds `slot()` a hole as it holds a parameter: as first built, a body filtering on its slot was refused unless it said `scans` (`slot_reaches_an_index_as_a_parameter_does`). Then `mark_slot` and `commit`, or `rollback`. The run is made durable after the lock as a write is: `flush` under `--sync always`, its replicas sent it before the next run, else `write_behind`; a failed sync is `Database::fail`'s, never retried. A run that lands and then finds the fence closed -- a lease lapsed mid-run -- is counted `unknown` and logged, its writes and mark landed (`a_panic_and_a_hook_s_refusal_are_marked_a_lapse_mid_run_is_not`).

*Missed slots and the clock.* A server down for a day runs the latest slot at its first look, the others `skipped` in the mark (`a_missed_slot_runs_at_the_first_look_and_the_shutdown_lands_it_whole`); a SIGTERM's one shutdown, taking the write lock, lands a run whole and synced or not at all. A mark ahead of the clock by at most `AHEAD_MAX` waits; further, the schedule goes on from the clock (`a_mark_ahead_of_the_clock_waits_an_hour_at_most`). Summer time runs as `due` has it (`summer_time_through_the_scheduler`). A crash before the fsync leaves the slot due in the file, run again once at the next open (`a_crash_before_the_fsync_runs_the_slot_again_once`).

*Who refused.* `state_refused` decides: `Error::Quota` and `Error::Io` are the state's, and `Error::ReadOnly` where the fence is closed or the database follows -- a hook's `ReadOnly` with the fence open is the statement's, told apart by asking the fence again. The state's refusal leaves the slot due, held in memory (`schedule::Held`: the slot, the status, since when, and the counter and limit it was tried at), tried again as the change counter or the limit moves, at most once a minute, or once a backoff doubling from a minute to `RETRY_MAX`, fifteen, ends; the next slot supersedes it, logged (`the_quota_holds_a_slot_and_the_next_supersedes_it`). Every other refusal is the statement's -- an unmet guard, a broken statement, a clash, a bound, a binding's or a hook's refusal, a panic, which is put back and marked 500 -- and marks the slot refused, its status (`api::status_of`) and `Error::reason` in the mark, landing in memory with the next block (`a_refusal_the_statement_makes_marks_its_slot`, `a_broken_statement_s_slots_are_marked_as_they_pass`). Each refused or held run's line carries an id of its own (`schedule::run_id`, a request id begun for the run).

*A run landing nothing.* No block, no fsync, no send: its mark waits in `Database::pending` (`a_run_that_wrote_nothing_lands_nothing`). The plan had the watcher told `Some(&[])`; as built nothing lands, so nothing is told, and what the plan guarded against is where the mark does land, with the next block: slice 2 noted it in the change ring as a schema change of no collection (`SCHEMA_MARK` on collection 0), which `changed_collections` reads as "everything", so every held write was woken, `waits`' epoch moved and every live query run again once a slot. A mark is now noted as `changes::SLOT_MARK` (`DocId::MAX` on collection 0), which `changed_collections` passes over and no collection's tail or `changes_since` reads, so a block carrying one names the collections its writes changed and no more -- `writes_in` and the counter as they were (`a_mark_names_no_collection_among_the_changes`, `a_held_claim_is_answered_by_a_run_s_write_never_by_its_mark`, which reads `waits::Stats::epoch`, added for it).

*Authority.* The server's token defines one; the audit log's `schema` line names its schedule beside the shape (`schedule`, the clause as written, `at '0 3 * * mon-fri' in 'Europe/Berlin'`, read off the text by `audit::schedule_of`; first built, the line parsed the definition again and wrote its schedule out by its meaning, `Schedule::written`, 3 KB of the server for a log line). The server's token calls one by hand (`Database::callee`'s `slot`, `Database::slot_at`): its body runs, its last statement answers, no slot is marked, and its `slot()` is the latest slot at or before the call (`a_call_by_hand_runs_the_slot_that_has_come_and_marks_none`, `a_scheduled_statement_is_the_server_token_s_to_call`). A scoped token's call is a missing statement's 404 at `/query`, `/batch` and `POST /_call/<name>`, a `call` line naming one is logged and grants nothing (fennec-server's `a_call_line_naming_a_scheduled_statement_is_logged`), MCP lists no tool (`a_scheduled_statement_is_no_tool`), and `/_schema` shows none to a scoped token, no name and no text (`/_whoami` repeats the policy's granted lines, as it does a binding named in one). A sync replica refuses the definition (`NAMED`), a step of its scenario.

*Docs and the upgrade.* FennecQL's reference has [Scheduled statements](/docs/fennecql#scheduled-statements); the functions page names them for work on a schedule, the tenants page that a node refuses one for now. RELEASING.md and the runbook's upgrade note say op 4 moves the format from this release on, and `make upgrade-check` gains a primary defining one over `/query` beside an old replica and an old archiver: the replica stops at the record in words and takes no write past it, the archiver takes no image, the old `fennec restore` refuses the archive this tree's restores, and the old server refuses the file it wrote; the fixture's `call lapse` now runs.

*Size and time.* The browser module against slice 2's head, `890ebce2`, this slice's base (`make size-report BASE=890ebce2`; main still lacked slice 2): raw and code the same to the byte with every feature, without the indexes (`fennec-lite`) and with none; gzip +1, +4 and +4, brotli -14, -99 and +36, each the report's noise. The scheduler is fennec-http's, and the engine's part -- `run_slot`, `slot_at`, `on_schedules`, the thread-local -- is the `statements` feature's, which the module is built without; `slot()`'s arm in `Registry::call` too, which first stood where the cfg left a reordered `match` behind it and cost the module 6 bytes (`Registry::call` +6), until the function's lookup went back to its shape and the arm went behind the cfg whole. `fennec-server`'s code (`make size-report BIN=fennec-server` here and at the base): 4 273.8 KB against 4 225.4, +48.4 KB (+1.15%), the binary that keeps its symbols 7 409.6 KB against 7 353.8 -- the scheduler's thread +22.3 KB, its loop with `look`, the run and the log lines inlined into the thread's body (counted as std's, the generic that starts a thread), and fennec-http's +5.8 beside it; fennec-core's +13.8, `run_slot` and its unwinding, `slot_at`, `callee`'s slot and `Schedule::written`; fennec-server's +4.6, the start's block. That is the feature itself, in the one binary that runs it; nothing of it reaches the module, the shell's, the native library's or a request's path.

What a request pays where no schedule is held: a write's block lands as it did, the `Database` holding one field more (`on_schedules`, read by `rebind` alone); a call of a function the table holds asks nothing more, the thread-local read only on a miss; and `changed_collections` walks the ring as it did, asking of its answer alone whether a collection 0 in it is slots' marks (`ChangeLog::slots_alone`, out of line, asked only where collection 0 changed). The three are measured by one script against main, in turns, seven pairs, each run made once the 1-minute load is under 1.5 and made again where it rose past 1.8 -- `write`, a `set` by id through `fennec_http::handle` over a file; `function`, `upper(s)` over 1 000 rows; `ring`, `changed_collections_since` 64 changes back -- the run pending a quiet machine, as slice 2's was.

*The review.* Each finding fixed with a test that fails with its fix taken out:

- *A `since` more than `AHEAD_MAX` ahead ran nothing, ever.* `due` took it for the time itself, read again at every look, so no slot was ever past it, with a mark or without, while the `clock` line said the schedule went on from the clock. A wrong reading now anchors nothing: with neither a mark nor `since` left, the latest slot runs once and its mark anchors the slots after it (`a_mark_ahead_of_the_clock_pauses_an_hour_at_most_then_goes_on_from_it`, `a_definition_a_year_ahead_runs_from_the_clock`).
- *A tenant node could hold a schedule nothing ran, without a word.* `PUT /_admin/tenants/<t>/file` (`Tenants::import_as`), and with it a move's target, took an image holding one. The install now refuses it (400, never the 409 a move reads as a file left at its target, the tenant left on its source), naming the statement and that a later release runs them there; a tenant whose file came holding one another way -- a `fennec restore` into the directory, a primary's stream -- is said to hold it unrun as it opens and as more arrive (`schedule::unrun`), as `--http-read-only` says it (`a_tenant_node_takes_no_scheduled_statement_yet`, fennec-server's `a_tenant_node_says_what_it_holds_unrun`).
- *A pending mark counted only past the landed mark's slot*, so behind a mark a wrong clock left ahead every run finding nothing ran the same slot at each look. Counted by order now, a run's mark landing in its block taking its name's pending one (`a_mark_left_ahead_outranks_no_run_after_it`).
- *`now()` was read once a look*: the sixteenth run of a look, behind fifteen fsyncs and their streams, read a stale `now()` and `alive`. The thread reads the clock once each run holds the write lock.
- *A panic outside a run's own catch ended the scheduler's thread silently*: now the one shutdown's, through `stop_with` (`a_panic_outside_a_run_stops_the_server`).
- *`slot()` reached no index*, so `where period = slot()` over `period @hash` was refused unless it said `scans`, and then walked the collection under the write lock each run: now handed to each filter as a value (`slot_reaches_an_index_as_a_parameter_does`), at 2.4 KB of the server.
- *A promoted replica waited up to a minute for its first slot*: `fork` tells the scheduler (`a_promoted_copy_runs_its_due_slot_at_once`); and an `--http-read-only` replica receiving a schedule says so as at its start (`a_read_only_replica_says_so_as_a_schedule_reaches_it`).
- *The docs*: the FennecQL reference gave the ahead line a run id it has not, the monitoring page lacked the `clock` event and the `schema` line's `schedule`, and `crates/fennec-ql/CLAUDE.md`'s rule ran a dozen sentences ([below](#what-claudemd-would-gain-from-f3)).

*Size, after the review.* The server's code (`make size-report BIN=fennec-server`, here and on main, `8b2d2c57`): 4 272.2 KB against 4 225.4, +46.8 KB (+1.11%); against the reviewed head's 4 273.8, -1.6 KB, with the fixes above in it. Cut: the audit line's `schedule` read off the lexer's tokens (`audit::schedule_of`) rather than the definition parsed again and written out by its meaning (`Schedule::written`, `cron_written`), -3.1 KB; `Look`, `Run` and `Ahead` and their strings built for `pass` alone, a test's, -1.7 KB of the thread's body; the counts by database, which only tests read before slice 5, gone; one `catch_unwind` a run where there were three, the clock and the slot given back by drop guards; and `run_slot` through the call's write loop (`run_callee`, `run_body`), one `bounded` instance fewer, -0.8 KB. Added: `slot()`'s index reach, 2.4 KB -- a `set` or a `del` made anew around its filter (`slot_handed`), as `Database::answered` makes one, `Statement`'s clone, every variant's, having cost 6.3 KB as first written -- `schedule::unrun` 0.9 KB, the thread's supervision 0.4 and the promotion's wake 0.3. The browser module against the reviewed head: raw and code the same to the byte in all three builds, gzip -4, -1 and -3, brotli -38, +226 and -123, the report's noise. A writing call's body now runs through `run_body`, shared with a scheduled run, whether a slot is handed asked once a call, outside the loop: the timing script measures `call bump {id: 1}` beside the three above.

*Time, after the review.* The timing script against main (`8b2d2c57`, seven pairs) found two: `ring` twice main's time, 194.7 ns to 388.3, and `call` +1.5%, 565.5 to 576.1. Each was judged by instructions retired (`/usr/bin/time -l`, a program a side running the one op through `fennec_http::handle` N times, less the same program running it none). The filter on the walk had made `changed_collections_since` 7 938 instructions a look 64 marks back against main's 3 480 -- a `Filter` has no exact length, so the `Vec` it collected into grew by doubling, five allocations where the walk's length had made one -- and asking the answer instead (`ChangeLog::slots_alone`, where collection 0 is in it) makes it 3 403, cycles 605 a look against main's 608. That keeps every consumer's answer: a slot's mark is always on collection 0, a replica's apply noting it there whatever its record's head says, and `a_slot_mark_names_no_collection` holds marks alone, among writes and beside a definition's. A writing call's instructions were main's to within 5 an op of some 9 800, before the fix and after, and its cycles within the runs' spread, built with `-C llvm-args=-align-all-functions=6` on every side or without; the branch a statement went outside the loop all the same, so no statement of a call asks it.

**Slice 4, tenant nodes.** *What it lets a user do:* define a scheduled statement on a tenant of a `fennec-server --dir` node -- by `/query`, `/batch`, a tenant created through the router with a schema declaring one, or an install or a move of a file holding one -- and have the node run it once a slot, whether the tenant is open or not, across a failover and a move.

*The scheduler* (`schedule::Node`, `schedule/node.rs`). Set on the registry by `Node::attach` before any tenant opens -- fennec-server's `serve_dir`, right after the listener is bound, before the standby's and the following tenants' opens -- and its thread, `fennec-schedule`, started where the endpoint's is, `stop_with(2)` where it cannot be made and the one shutdown's should it end; a node under `--http-read-only` attaches none, its tenants saying what they hold unrun as they open (`schedule::unrun`, "this node runs no scheduler"), a definition refused (`SCHEDULED_UNRUN`) and an install of an image holding one refused (400, never the 409 a move reads as a file left). The queue is by name: each scheduled tenant's entry -- its next instant, and the state its looks keep (held slots, marks told ahead), taken by a look and put back after -- a binary heap of `(instant, name)` for the tenants waiting, each name an `Arc<str>` its entry shares, an item whose tenant's instant moved or which left passed over as it comes, and a `VecDeque` of the tenants due, never a tenant's `Arc`; the plan's queue named a statement too, which a tenant's look finds for itself (`Database::due`), so an entry is a tenant. The thread sleeps until the first instant, a minute at most, and with none -- a node holding no schedule -- on a condition with no deadline: it wakes for nothing, as a file's server holding none does.

*A look* (`Node::look`, a test's `Node::pass(now)`). The tenant through the registry (`Tenants::for_run`): opened where closed -- unless its lease lets the node write it no more, when it stays closed and is looked at again within a minute, never opened to find so --, under its slot's lock, the memory ceiling and its `<file>.lock`, as a request opens it, with its `last_used` left as it was -- `UNUSED`, 0, where the look opened it, a request's never 0 (`Tenants::now` counts from 1) -- so a client's open evicts it first, and its own open evicts only tenants no client used (`make_room(_, run)`). Then the tenant's gate held as a request holds it (`Tenant::enter`), a frozen tenant passed over and looked at again a minute on or as it thaws (`Tenants::thaw` wakes it), and `look_at` -- slice 3's look, given the tenant's database and `most`, 1: one slot run, a tenant with another due going behind the tenants due (`again`), so each tenant due runs once before any twice. Then `Tenants::close_after_run` closes it where the look opened it and no request used it since, its `last_used` still `UNUSED`, its next look where a held slot's backoff ends (`look_at`'s closed instant), nothing moving a closed tenant's counter or limit; one a request opened meanwhile closes as any tenant does, idle. The lease, the fence and the lapse after a landing are slice 3's, the tenant's database holding its node's fence (`set_fence`); the quota is its `set_limit`'s. A run is charged no rate: `quota::limit` charged every landing's bytes to the tenant's bytes a second, the sweep's and a compact's too, while the rows a second were a request's own -- the design's rule (`writing_here`, [quotas.md](quotas.md#tenant-quotas)), which the bytes now keep as well, so a tenant's clients are not slowed by its schedule (`a_run_charges_no_rate`). A following copy waits for nothing, looked at again as its promotion tells it (`Database::fork`) -- slice 3 looked again each minute, ten thousand looks a minute on a standby node for nothing.

*The hint* (`schedule/hint.rs`, `schedules.hint` beside the tenants' files). A line a tenant: its name, its next instant or `-` to look at once, and for one closed with marks pending where its file stood -- its change, its history's id and its forks (`Stood`) -- and each mark, `<statement>,<slot>,<version>,<skipped>,<status>,<reason in hex>`. Entered, synced, a line appended (`hint::append`, `Node::note`): before every scheduled definition writes anything, again where the tenant is named already (`Database::on_schedules`' hook, one a tenant's open makes, asked with `None` in `define_statement` after `SCHEDULED_UNRUN`, refusing the definition with what it answers -- `Error::Io` where the hint could not be written); before an installed image holding one is renamed into place (`Tenants::import_as`, a move's target), its first look queued only once it is in place, under its slot, and the thread woken (`Node::installed`); as a tenant opens holding one (`Node::opened` -- a file restored into the directory behind the node, a copy that took its primary's schedules); and as the engine says the table came to hold one (`on_schedules`: a replica's apply, an image adopted, a promotion). Left as a tenant closes holding none, its file synced by the close first (`Tenants::close_with`) and a failed sync leaving it named (`Node::closed`, after the last `Arc` went), so the hint names a tenant whose last drop is not yet durable rather than miss one. Written whole by rename at most once a minute where it changed, from the thread; the plan had the whole unsynced and only an entering synced, but a line appended to a file whose rename the directory had not kept goes with the rename at a power loss, so each whole write is synced, file and directory, at most a minute's fsync. A crash cutting an append short leaves a last line with no newline, passed over -- a definition that never landed -- and cut off by the next append before it writes (`hint::append`; ended with a newline instead, the piece would be a line that does not read), the start owing a whole write; any other line that does not read makes the hint none. With none at the start -- the first start running slice 4, or a hint removed -- the scheduler's thread first opens each tenant once, one at a time (`Node::learn`), saying so as it begins and how many hold one as it ends, and only then writes the hint: until it is written the entering is memory's, so a crash while learning learns again. A start with a hint opens no tenant before its slot and none that holds no schedule (`a_start_opens_no_tenant_before_its_slot_and_a_hint_removed_is_learned_again`).

*A move's source.* Frozen, it runs nothing, its lease's fence aside. Deleted after the flip, it leaves the hint (`Node::forget`); a delete refused because a request still holds it leaves the tenant open and frozen, and one failing once it closed the tenant -- its identity not put away, its file not removed, a reclaim keeping it -- leaves it closed and its freeze gone with it, and only its freeze, which is memory's, kept it from running: a restart would find it named in the hint and run its slots into a file no router sends to, writing on the history its target carries on. So such a delete takes it out of the hint, written whole at once (`Node::left`, `Tenants::left_frozen`; `a_frozen_tenant_whose_delete_fails_leaves_the_hint`), and a thaw enters it again (`Node::woken`); the router takes the file back by its identity as before. What is left is a restart of a node without `--lease` that opens the file for something else -- a start with no hint, which opens every file -- as any writer reaching a file left would; with a lease the fence refuses it, the lease naming the tenant's new node (`a_moved_tenant_runs_its_slot_on_its_target_once_and_its_frozen_source_none`).

*Pending marks across a close.* A tenant closing tells the scheduler, under its slot, what its database holds (`schedule::closing`): its pending marks, where its file stands and its next slot, computed then, so a start or a look opens it no sooner. Kept by name in the entry and the hint, they are handed back as the tenant opens (`Database::keep_pending`) only where its file stands where it stood -- the same change, the same history and forks -- and dropped otherwise: a write since, or another file in its place, and the slot runs again over what is there (`a_closed_tenant_s_pending_mark_is_kept_until_its_file_changes`). Lost with the hint, a pending mark costs a run made again over the data the lost one read, as slice 3 has it. Without them each closed tenant's empty runs were made again at every open, an idle tenant opened each minute for a purge of nothing. The shutdown keeps the open tenants' as a close would (`Tenants::shutdown_with`, `Node::shutdown`).

*The router* reads a schema declaring a schedule with the grammar it was built with, as F1 had it, and sends it to the node, which defines it as it applies the rest: no code changed, held by fennec-shard's `scheduled.rs`, whose tenants are created that way.

*Tests*, each failing with its part taken out. fennec-http's `node_scheduled.rs`: `a_closed_tenant_opens_as_its_slot_comes_and_closes_after_its_run`, `a_run_is_no_use_of_a_tenant_and_evicts_no_hot_one`, `a_start_opens_no_tenant_before_its_slot_and_a_hint_removed_is_learned_again`, `a_tenant_enters_the_hint_before_its_first_schedule_lands` (a definition put back with its block leaves the tenant named on disk; a start after that opens it once and it leaves the hint), `a_definition_the_hint_cannot_name_is_refused` (refused, nothing written, where the hint cannot take its line), `a_tenant_installed_or_restored_in_is_learned`, `a_thousand_tenants_due_at_once_each_run_once_before_any_twice`, `a_closed_tenant_s_pending_mark_is_kept_until_its_file_changes`, `a_frozen_tenant_runs_nothing_and_thawed_runs_its_slot`, `a_run_at_its_tenant_s_quota_is_held_until_room_comes` (a run at the rows' quota held 507 and run once a delete made room, a purge let through, a client's purge landing a pending mark), `a_run_charges_no_rate`; `hint`'s `a_hint_reads_back_what_was_written_and_its_appends`; `tenants.rs`' `a_node_running_no_scheduler_takes_no_scheduled_statement` (slice 3's, renamed). fennec-shard's `scheduled.rs`, three leased nodes behind proxies and a router, in `lease_nodes.rs`' shape (whose nodes now attach a scheduler, as `fennec-server --dir` does): `a_run_its_stream_never_sent_runs_once_on_the_promoted_copy` (the old node cut off after its run, the promoted copy running that slot once, `slot()` the same, the old node fenced past its lease and, back, following the promoted copy with its run gone), `a_run_its_stream_sent_is_not_run_again_on_the_promoted_copy`, `a_moved_tenant_runs_its_slot_on_its_target_once_and_its_frozen_source_none`, `a_move_undone_leaves_its_source_to_run_the_slot`. fennec-server's `a_node_learns_its_hint_and_runs_a_tenant_s_missed_slot` and `a_read_only_node_says_what_it_holds_unrun` (slice 3's `a_tenant_node_says_what_it_holds_unrun`, now a node under `--http-read-only`).

*Lateness* (`make schedule-bench`, `examples/schedules.rs`): ten thousand tenants of a hundred rows each, closed, each with a statement due at one slot, run by one look at that instant, in process on an M1 at a 1-minute load under 1.5. A purge finding nothing -- its mark pending, kept as the tenant closes -- ran the ten thousand in 1.54 s: the median run 725 ms behind its slot, the p99 1.50 s, the last 1.52 s, 6 483 tenants opened a second, the files no byte longer. An insert ran them in 53.3 s, the median 26.6 s late and the last 53.3 s, 188 tenants a second, 39 bytes a tenant; under `--sync always` 53.6 s. Each writing tenant's file is synced once -- at its close under an interval `--sync`, as its run lands under `--sync always`, the close then finding nothing to sync -- and a sync here is `F_FULLFSYNC`'s flush of the drive, 5.3 ms of the 5.35 a writing tenant took: the scheduler's own share is the purge's 0.15 ms. A start with no hint learned the ten thousand in 1.2 s, four at a time; one at a time, as the review left it, 2.3 s (below). So ten thousand tenants each writing at one instant run within a minute on this machine, under the bound the risks set; past it what is owed is the syncs beside the scheduler -- a close handed to threads of its own, a durable run's wait for its sync apart from the next tenant's run -- the drive's time and not the thread's, which the pool the risks name would not shorten alone. Linux's durable run syncs the log beside the file (`synclog.rs`), not measured here.

*Size and time.* The browser module does not move (`make size-report BASE=origin/main`: 818.2 KB raw to the byte, gzip -7 bytes and brotli +12, `fennec-lite` and the build with no feature the same raw): what fennec-core gains is behind `statements`, which the module leaves out. `fennec-server`'s code grows 58.5 KB, 4 283.1 KB to 4 341.6 (+1.4%, `make size-report BIN=fennec-server` on each tree): `schedule::node` 36.9 KB -- the queue, the looks, the learning's threads, the hint read inside `attach` --, `look_at` 7.4 KB, out of line and the file's watcher's too (its thread 17.6 KB less), `hint` 2.8 KB, the queue's ordered set and the maps' tables about 6 KB, `serve_dir` 3.2 KB and the registry's hooks about 7 KB (`close_after_run`, and in `close_with`, `shutdown_with`, `thaw`, `delete_held` and `import_as`). A node holding no schedule runs none of it but the hooks: two closures and a map's look as a tenant opens, a map's look as it closes. Its time, by instructions retired (`/usr/bin/time -l`; a program a side running one part through the registry and `fennec_http::handle`, less the same program running none, twice each): a put by id 18 511 and 18 582 an op against main's 18 584 and 18 416, a get by id 14 277 and 14 226 against 14 085 and 14 355 -- each inside the runs' own spread, a percent and more either way --, a tenant's open and close 1.298 M and 1.302 M against 1.302 M and 1.304 M, and an open under `--max-memory` closing the tenant before it 1.340 M and 1.347 M against 1.338 M and 1.340 M (+0.1% and +0.5%): the hooks' closures and map looks, a thousandth of what an open costs. A node's start, its hint written whole and synced, ran 8.5 M instructions more, once. `make shard-bench ARGS=latency`, six runs a side in ABBA turns at loads of 1.29-1.63: a request through the router 41.7 us p50 against main's 41.75 (41.6-42.6 and 41.5-42.2), the node asked directly 21.7 against 21.8 (21.6-22.0 both), the router's added 20.0 against 20.05.

*After the review.* An install's first look was queued before its file was renamed into place, outside the slot: a look in between found no file, forgot the tenant and the next whole write dropped it from the hint -- never run, a 64 MB image's write the window -- and with no word to the thread a node holding no other schedule slept through it, a move of an idle tenant onto it never run. The line is still appended, synced, before the rename (`Node::note`); the look is queued once the file is there, under the slot a look's open waits on, and the thread woken (`Node::installed`), and a look finding no file forgets the tenant only where nothing entered it since the look began (`an_install_racing_a_looking_scheduler_runs_and_stays_named`, its thread running beside a loop of looks; `an_install_or_a_move_in_wakes_a_sleeping_scheduler`, the thread asleep with no deadline). Every scheduled definition appends its line, not a tenant's first alone: a yearly schedule replaced by an hourly one left the hint's next at the new year until the next whole write, a minute at most, and a crash in that minute read it (`a_replaced_schedule_is_looked_at_at_once_after_a_crash`); definitions are rare, an fsync each. An installed tenant is first looked at `ARRIVING`, a minute, after its install (`an_installed_tenant_runs_nothing_until_it_arrived_and_one_taken_back_none`): a node without a lease never learns of a move's flip, and a move undone -- its quota or its placement refused -- takes the target's file back within the move's own call, so a target run at once ran a slot its thawed source then ran again. Waiting for the flip itself wanted a word from the router no router sends, or a hook on a client's first request on every request's path; a slot due on an installed tenant runs that minute late, once, and what is left is a take-back that fails, the file left a minute -- the risk any file left carries. A closed tenant the node's lease does not name is not opened for its slot (`Tenants::for_run`'s `run`), and a closed tenant's held slot is looked at again as its backoff ends, a minute doubling to fifteen (`look_at`'s closed instant), not each minute: a node whose lease lapsed, or whose tenants sat at their quotas, opened each of them every minute to find nothing to run (`a_node_without_its_lease_opens_no_tenant_for_its_slot`, `a_closed_tenant_s_held_slot_waits_its_backoff_out`); the learning opens under no lease, as a leased node's start holds none yet. A pending mark handed back no later than its name's landed mark is dropped (`Database::keep_pending`; fennec-core's `a_pending_mark_handed_back_counts_only_past_the_landed_one`), a pending mark outranking the landed one by order. A frozen tenant whose delete fails at any step, its freeze gone with its close, or whom a reclaim keeps, leaves the hint as a refused delete's does (`Tenants::left_frozen`; `a_frozen_tenant_whose_delete_fails_leaves_the_hint`, its identity's sidecar a directory so the put-away fails; a reclaim's second judgment has no such hook, the same path by its code). A close syncs before telling the scheduler and a failed sync leaves a tenant holding none named, its drop's sync having only logged. One tenant's panic is its own ([decision 9](#f3s-decisions-for-the-owner); `a_look_s_panic_holds_its_tenant_back_and_one_outside_a_look_stops_the_node`, on the thread). A torn last line is cut off by the next append before it writes, and a hint read past one written whole at the first chance.

*Bytes and time, after the review.* `fennec-server`'s code is 4 325.6 KB, 16.0 KB less than the slice's first 4 341.6 and 42.5 KB over main's 4 283.1 (`make size-report BIN=fennec-server`, by module against the first): `schedule::node` 3.3 KB less with the per-tenant catch, the arrival and the lease's look in it -- each entry's look state in the entry, a second map by name gone, and one hook a tenant's open makes where two closures and two copies of its name were; the B-tree's code 3.1 KB, the waiting set a binary heap whose items a moved or departed tenant leaves stale, passed over as they come, where an ordered set's removal was a name's copy; drop glue 2.6 KB and iterator adapters 2.5 KB, `collect` into an `Option<Vec>` written as loops and the hint read into the queue's own entries rather than a map moved there; `serve_dir` 1.5 KB, the learning's thread and its four scoped ones gone -- the scheduler's thread learns, one tenant at a time, before its first look -- ten thousand tenants in 2.1 to 2.35 s against four threads' 1.06 to 1.24, once at a start with no hint, its runs waiting that long; the purge's ten thousand at one slot then ran in 1.41 to 1.60 s against the first's 1.44 to 1.55, eight runs a side in turns at loads near 2, a look's instructions and cycles a tenant the same (`proc_pid_rusage` around the pass: 1.64 to 1.76 M and 425 to 476 thousand against 1.65 to 1.74 M and 442 to 464 thousand); `hint` 1.4 KB, its text written under the queue's lock with no line copied, twice before.
