| the key held with another body | as the first, logged, `conflict` counted |
| no statement consumes the hook | 409 |
| a statement of the hook unread; an event a broken statement's condition takes | 409, naming the hook |
| no condition takes the event | 200, `{"taken": 0}` |
| a parameter missing or of another type; `check` false | 400, naming the parameter and its path |
| `require` unmet | 412, its `reason` |
| `insert` over a held id, a `@unique` value taken | 409 |
| past `--call-max-rows` or `MAX_WOKEN` | 400, naming the bound |
| a binding's refusal | its status, its `reason` |
| the tenant's bytes or rows; above `--max-memory`, statements that insert or set | 507; the tenant's rate, 429 with `Retry-After` |
| a lease lapsed after the landing | 500, `outcome: unknown` |
| a storage error | 500 |
| taken | 200, `{"event": "<key>", "taken": <statements>}`, `Fennec-Seq` |

Never a 410, which tells a Standard Webhooks sender to "disable the webhook endpoint", and never a 3xx, which Stripe counts a failure: `/_hooks/` answers neither on any path. Every 503 says when to come back, in its body too ([quotas.md](quotas.md#retry-after-in-bodies)), though no sender above reads it; each retries on its own schedule.

**A sender is told what a scoped caller is told.** A sender is another company's server, and what it is answered lands in a delivery log its staff read: the status, the guard's `reason` -- the definer's words, written to be told -- and the bound a refusal names, never the engine's words, which name values ("document 3 holds `bob@x.io` already"), nor a statement's name. That is `told_call`'s rule for a scoped token's call ([authority](#authority)); the words and the name are the server's log line's and the trace's, under the request id and beside the event's key.

**The 2xx waits for the disk, whatever `--sync` says.** A client told a buffered write landed can read it back and write it again; a sender told 2xx never sends the event again. So an event's block is made durable before it is answered, as a write under `--sync always` is -- its fsync after the lock and the streams keeping up (`Feed::wait_sent`, `SENT_WAIT` at most) -- on a server whose other writes are buffered ([decision 4](#f4s-decisions-for-the-owner)): a crash 200 ms after a buffered 200 would lose a payment no one tells fennecdb of again. An event taken by no condition writes nothing and waits for nothing. What it costs the buffered writers beside it is measured ([what a hook costs](#what-a-hook-costs)).

**Where the tenant has a standby, the 2xx may wait for one to hold the event** -- the review's proposal, recommended under [decision 4](#f4s-decisions-for-the-owner) and the owner's to take. A durable write waits for every stream keeping up and passes a lagging one over, which is the failover table's last row: told 200, lost with the primary. Since a sender's retries are the queue, a hook can afford what a client cannot: its 2xx waits for a stream a promotion could take the event from -- the pair's standby or the tenant's `--replicas` copy, never a read replica, which a failover never promotes, nor an archive -- to have sent the block, `Feed::wait_sent`'s meaning of a stream keeping up, the lagging one waited for too, bounded by `SENT_WAIT`; past it the answer is 503 with `Retry-After`, the block landed and its key kept, so the sender's next try is answered from the key once a standby holds it, or taken once by the standby promoted meanwhile. The cost is latency alone -- a standby's round of the stream, measured in its slice -- and, with every standby gone, 503s the sender retries until the router attaches another, which `fennec_router_tenants_unreplicated` counts already. What a durable write's wait cannot reach it cannot either: a socket's bytes reach the standby after the process is killed, not after the machine dies. On a file's server the streams a promotion could take it from are its followers'; a tenant with no standby, and a server with no follower, answer after the fsync.

### Stripe's events, as Stripe sends them

The review read Stripe's documentation for what a hook's statements and its setup owe beyond the signature; each is the reference page's to say, with an example written so.

- *Snapshot payloads.* Stripe's Dashboard gives an event destination a payload style, Thin or Snapshot. A thin event carries its object's id and no `data.object`, and is read by fetching the object from Stripe's API -- a call out, which a hook never makes ([below](#what-a-hook-never-does)). So a `stripe` hook takes Snapshot events: the reference page says to choose Snapshot, and a thin event meeting a statement whose parameters read `data.object.*` is refused 400 naming the path, loud in Stripe's delivery log. Thin events are left ([decision 13](#f4s-decisions-for-the-owner)).
- *Two events for one change.* "In some cases, two separate Event objects are generated and sent", each with its own `id`, so keying by `id` takes both. A statement's guards are written idempotent by the object's id (`data.object.id`) -- `stripe_paid`'s `intent = $intent` is -- or its write is an `insert ... if absent` keyed by it; otherwise the second event is refused, a held id's or a `@unique` value's 409 or a guard's 412, for the three days Stripe retries it.
- *Test and live modes.* Each signs with a secret of its own, at an endpoint of its own: a mode is a hook (`stripe`, `stripe_test`), and the live hook's statements read `event.livemode = true` too, so a test secret set on the live hook by mistake writes nothing. Both secrets in one hook's two slots would leave none for a rotation and take test events into production.
- *Addresses.* Stripe now advises allowlisting the addresses it sends from beside verifying signatures ([IP addresses](https://docs.stripe.com/ips)). fennecdb keeps no list ([below](#what-a-hook-never-does)); a firewall in front may. Behind the global entry the Worker is what a sender reaches and a router sees Cloudflare's addresses, so only a Cloudflare rule on the client's address -- the one `CF-Connecting-IP` carries, `ip.src` in a rule -- over `/t/<t>/_hooks/stripe` can hold a hook to Stripe's ranges; the reference and the sharding pages say how.

### A hook's authority

**The definition's, by the server's token, and no token's.** An event has no token: its sender proved it holds the secret, and that is all it is. Its statements run their text as a call by the server's token runs one, checked by no scope, with no claim -- as F3's runs do ([a run's authority](#a-runs-authority)) -- so what an event can do is what its statements' text says, fixed when they were defined, and what it can choose is the values its parameters read and nothing else, never a name, a filter or a statement. The sender is to a hook what the writer is to a binding: whatever its parameters read, the sender chooses, and a statement's guards are where its definer holds them to the data, as `stripe_paid`'s total is held ([F2](#a-bindings-authority): `new.` is the writer's document). Who may define one is who may define any statement: the server's token, never a scoped token, and no grant lets one -- a statement an outside party's events run, unscoped, for ever, is the elevation F1's definer model keeps out.

**No token calls one by name but the server's.** A `call` line naming a hook's statement is logged as the server starts and grants nothing; a scoped token's call of one is the 404 a missing one is; MCP lists no tool for it. The server's token may `call` one with an event as its argument -- `call stripe_paid $1`, `$1` the event's object -- the studio's "send a test event" and an operator's test: its condition asked, its parameters read from the event as a delivery's are, its body run, its answer the run's, with no signature asked, no key kept and `hook.id`, `hook.at` and `hook.event` `null`. The native library's embedder, in the server token's place, calls it alike. A test event a sender sends -- Stripe's `stripe trigger`, GitHub's ping -- is a delivery, verified and keyed as any.

**Its runs wake bindings and are held to the bounds and the tenant's rates.** Its writes go through `run_one` and wake the bindings they reach, no `wake` line asked, the server's token waking every binding. An event examines at most `--call-max-rows` rows, one budget for every statement it wakes and their bindings, fresh an event; `MAX_WOKEN` holds its woken rows. A hook is a request to its tenant: its writes are charged to the tenant's rates on its own thread (`writing_here`, 429), and its block to the tenant's bytes and rows as it lands (507) -- each a refusal the sender's backoff waits out. Stripe warns of "a large spike in webhook deliveries (for example, during the beginning of the month when all subscriptions renew)"; a tenant's rate is the flow control, and Stripe's retries the queue. `--call-rate` holds a subject's calls, and an event has none.

**`route_tenant` asks the signature in a token's place.** "No route below `/t/<t>/` may skip" `route_tenant` ([access.md](access.md#tenant-binding)), and a hook does not: as a tenant's replication stream is asked the node's replication token there rather than a data token, a hook is asked its signature against the tenant's secret there, before the tenant is looked up. On a file's server `POST /_hooks/<name>` is judged before `authenticate` asks for a token, and is the one route a server holding `--http-token` runs a statement for without one -- which is why nothing of it is read before its signature checks out. A server with no hooks file has no such route: its `/_hooks/` asks for a token as every unknown path does.

**Audited as a definition; an event is in the file.** `create statement ... on hook`, its replacement and its drop are schema changes logged by their shapes (`audit::statement`), the line naming the hook; a tenant's secret set or taken away is an admin event naming the tenant and the hook, and a set read through `hook-secrets` one naming the tenant. A taken event is no audit event, as a write is none: its key's row and its writes are in the file, replicated, archived and restored together. A refused delivery is a 401's audit line or a log line ([below](#watching-hooks)).

### A hook's record, replicas and upgrades

**Kind 13, op 6: a hook's definition, which a binary from before refuses.** `[13][0][length][6][history][change][epoch][digest][name][depends][text][hook]`: op 1's fields and the text, then the hook's name, the record's tail, so a build that cannot parse the text knows which hook to refuse (above) and the module keeps the tail as the bytes it is, as op 4's schedule is kept. F3's reader refuses an op it does not read as corrupt (`engine/stored.rs`), so an F3 binary -- a replica, an archiver, a node a tenant moves to, a standby a failover would promote -- stops at the first hook in words ([storage.md](storage.md#file-format)). Read as op 1, it would be a callable statement whose text does not read: marked broken, and the promoted node answering the hook's sender 404 for days. Op 6 has no mark beside it: an event's trace is its key's row and its writes, rows any binary reads.

**`writes_in` counts it as one write**, so a replica's counter stays its primary's; an image writes it with op 6, a compact keeps it, `Undo::Statements` puts it back, a replica applies it and runs nothing, and `/_changes` passes over it and tells an event's writes in its block's change range, its key's row passed over. A drop (op 2) takes the definition; the keys stay until their `until`, so a statement defined again under the hook takes no event already taken.

**Its dependencies and its contract are F1's.** `depends` names what the body reads and writes; a schema change breaking it is refused at its commit, naming it, and an internal writer marks it broken, which refuses the events its condition takes ([above](#where-an-event-is-taken-and-once)). A replacement keeps the kind -- a callable, bound or scheduled statement replaced by a hook's, or the other way, is refused naming the `drop` and `create` it needs -- and changes its hook, condition, parameters and body freely, no grant hanging on them. The canonical tree writes the hook's clauses only where they hold something, so no stored digest moves, `GRAMMAR_EPOCH` stays where it is, and `make fennecql-corpus` holds every FennecQL text of the repository to main's tree.

**Declared in code**, as every statement: `schema.fennecql` may hold one, `declared::plan` plans it as any statement's `create` or `create or replace`, and `integrations/schema-golden.json` gains the case.

**Upgrades go one way, every reader before the first hook**: F1's order ([upgrades](#replicas-sync-the-router-and-upgrades)) -- archivers, routers, every node, and then a primary may define one. `make upgrade-check` gains a base, the last main before op 6, whose replica stops at op 6 in words and connects again, whose archiver keeps the stream's bytes and takes no image of its own, whose `restore` and server refuse the archive and the file, whose router refuses a schema declaring a hook (400, nothing placed), and onto whose node a move fails at the install, the tenant left on its source. RELEASING.md's notes name `on hook` as the clause that moves the format.

### Hooks on tenant nodes, the router and the global entry

**A tenant's hook is `/t/<tenant>/_hooks/<name>`, verified by the node against that tenant's secret.** Behind the global entry, `https://<entry>/t/acme/_hooks/stripe` is what Stripe is told: the Worker serves every `/t/<tenant>/` path ([regions.md](regions.md#the-global-entry)), drops every address the sender claims and names it in `Fennec-Client`, and a router believing the entry (`--entry-token`) counts, waits out and logs the sender's refusals by that address, in the hooks' own count ([above](#verifying-an-event)). Neither the Worker nor a router changes a byte of the body or of a header a scheme reads -- a signature is over the raw bytes. A body sent with no length is read where it arrives: the router reads a chunked body itself (`http::read_request`), and the global entry answers 411 only to a chunked body past a mebibyte, which it would hold whole for a second router (`BUFFER_AT`, `integrations/cloudflare/entry/entry.js`) -- the first draft's "the router's 411" cited a refusal the router does not make. Stripe, GitHub and Svix send a `Content-Length`.

**The router forwards a hook as bytes, and takes it as keyed.** A `POST` the router may have sent whole goes again only where `replayable` allows ([router.md](router.md#pooled-connections)): a `GET`, `HEAD`, `OPTIONS`, a keyed or a marked request -- keyed by its `Idempotency-Key` header, which no sender sends. A hook is keyed by its event at the node, its key landing in its block and a second try answered from it, so `replayable` takes a `POST` to `/t/<t>/_hooks/` as keyed, by its path, and the entry's rule, the router's own, takes it alike. The node is the one to say whether it ran, as it is for a keyed write; the router parses no body, its check a segment of the path it reads the tenant from already.

**The router relays a tenant's secrets and keeps none.** `PUT /_shard/tenants/<t>/hooks/<name>` (the router's admin token) takes the tenant's claim, as a quota's `PUT` does (`claim`), and goes to the tenant's node as `/_admin/tenants/<t>/hooks/<name>`, relayed once across regions (`regional_admin`): a node refusing it records nothing; then the directory records the hook's name and a new version, and the router copies the set to each standby -- the pair's and the tenant's `--replicas` copy -- through `hook-secrets` ([above](#where-the-secret-lives)), read from the primary and installed whole. It answers once the primary and every standby hold the version: 200 with each copy that did not take it named (`behind`), counted by `fennec_router_tenants_hooks_behind` and copied again at the router's repair, never a 2xx that hides a gap -- a standby promoted without the set cannot be given it once its primary is gone, and answers the sender 401 until an operator sets it again. `GET /_shard/tenants/<t>/hooks` lists what the node lists. Where a copy lacks the version -- a standby attached, a follow begun (which put away the copy's own set, [above](#where-the-secret-lives)), a failed attach tried again (`try_again`), a move's target, a copy across regions (`move_across`, each step undone where the next fails) -- the router installs it on that copy the same way, before the flip for a move, as it sends a tenant's quota there ([quotas.md](quotas.md#tenant-quotas)); a failover's promoted copy already holds it, and keeps it. **A `PUT` is ordered against a running move**: a move holds the tenant's claim from its start to its end (`relocate`, `move_across`), so a `PUT` meanwhile is refused 409 ("another operation on `acme` is in progress"), never landing on the source between the copy of its set and the flip, where the target would come up without it; the move copies the set before the flip under the same claim. A read replica holds none: it answers marked reads alone, and a hook is a write. The secrets are in the router's memory only as they pass; a directory archive holds names and versions.

**A forged event opens no tenant.** The node verifies against the secrets in its memory before `tenants.get` opens the tenant, so an unauthenticated sender cannot make a node open its closed tenants one after another, past `--idle-close` and `--max-memory`. A verified event opens its tenant as any request does, and counts as its use.

**A query across tenants takes no event**, reading; the router's schema parse (`admin::schema_description`) takes a schema declaring a hook with the grammar, as it takes a binding and a schedule.

### Hooks beside bindings, schedules, held writes, keys and quotas

- *Bindings*: an event's writes wake them, in its block ([above](#a-hooks-authority)) -- `stripe_paid` and `paid_ledger` are a payment and its ledger line as one record. A binding refusing refuses the event, its `reason` told.
- *Schedules*: a hook acts on what another service says, a schedule on time; neither runs the other. A body reaping events never confirmed -- a reservation Stripe never paid -- is a schedule's, reading the rows hooks wrote.
- *Held writes*: an event's writes wake the held writes reading what they wrote (`Waits::wrote`, [waits.md](waits.md#waking-held-writes)), so a worker holding `claim` on `jobs` is answered when a hook inserts a job -- the one-request worker queue F1's example builds, fed by another company's events. An event is never held: `Fennec-Wait` on a hook is refused (400), and one taken by no condition tells the watcher nothing.
- *`Idempotency-Key`*: not read on a hook; its event is its key. The machinery is shared ([above](#where-an-event-is-taken-and-once)), the replay's wait for a durable position with it, which `Idempotency-Key` gains first ([above](#a-replay-waits-for-what-its-first-try-waited-for)).
- *Quotas*: an event's block is held to the tenant's bytes and rows as it lands and its writes to the tenant's rates; its key's row is the server's, never the tenant's rows, though its bytes are the file's.
- *`--max-memory`*: a hook whose taking statements insert or set is held as a call of that body is (`over_ceiling`), 507 above the ceiling, one that only deletes going on.

### Hooks beside sync replicas, the module and the native library

**A sync replica defines none and takes none; what an event wrote reaches it through its shapes.** `NAMED` refuses `create statement ... on hook` on a synced database as it refuses every definition; `integrations/sync-scenarios.json` gains the refusal before either runner changes ([native-and-sync.md](native-and-sync.md#sync-scenarios)), and an event's rows come through the shapes following their collections as any server write's do.

**The module keeps the record and takes no event.** Op 6 is core, as op 1's, 3's and 4's records are: every build reads it, keeps it through its images and lists the statement, its hook's name the tail it keeps unread. The module has no HTTP route to take an event at and refuses defining and calling already ("named statements are a server's", [the browser module](#the-browser-module-and-embedded)); a hook binds no write, so where F2 refuses a bound write in the module, F4 refuses nothing -- a page or the shell holding a server's file (`openFile`) takes no event, and its sender retries until a server serves the file again. Measured in its slice (`make size-report BASE=origin/main`, all three builds), against the 824 bytes brotli op 1's record cost.

**The native library takes none.** An app on a phone has no address a sender can reach; an embedder running a server of its own runs `fennec-server`. It keeps the record and calls a hook's statement with an event as the server's token does (above).

### What a hook never does

- *No call out.* No acknowledgement back to the sender, no fetch of the event or its object from the sender's API to check it -- Stripe suggests one ("retrieve the API resource") -- no DNS lookup of the sender: the event as signed is all a hook reads, and a socket waited on under the write lock would stall every writer, the reason F1's statements make none ([what it does not do](#what-it-does-not-do)).
- *No user code.* The statement is FennecQL, parsed once; a scheme is one of three, written in fennec-http.
- *No network wait under the lock.* The body's read, the signature, the parse, the conditions and the typing come before it; under it, the key, the runs and the key's row; the fsync and the streams after it.
- *No reading before verifying*, and no answer telling what exists before it.
- *No rows to a sender*, no secret in the file, an archive, a log, a trace, a metric or an answer, and no response computed from the event -- a challenge to echo, as Slack's `url_verification` asks ([also left](#webhook-consumers-against-other-systems)).
- *No IP allowlist of its own.* Stripe and GitHub publish the addresses they send from, and Stripe now advises holding a hook to them beside its signature; fennecdb's authority is the signature, which an address cannot forge and a list kept in a server goes stale against, so the list is the network's: a firewall in front, and behind the global entry a Cloudflare rule on the client's address ([above](#stripes-events-as-stripe-sends-them)), the one place that address is still the sender's.

### Watching hooks

- *The listings*: `statements` gains `hook` (its name, or `null`) and `armed` (whether this server, or this tenant on its node, holds a secret for it); `describe statement` the condition and each parameter's path; `GET /_schema` to the server's token, `?as=fennecql` writing them after the collections. A scoped token sees none.
- *The log*: a refused or conflicting event writes a line naming the hook, the event's key, the status, the statement that refused and the engine's words -- what the sender was not told -- and a 401 the audit's refusal line naming its check; GitHub's `X-GitHub-Delivery` beside the key. Never a body, a signature or a secret. An event past `--slow-ms` writes the slow line, `hook: ["stripe", [["stripe_paid", [h, c], rows]]]`.
- *Traces*: an event is its request's trace ([http.md](http.md#traces)): its span's `db.operation.name` `hook`, a child a taking statement with `db.stored_procedure.name` its name, the outcome; never the event's key or a value of its body, as a span names no value.
- *`/_stats/statements`* counts each taking statement as a shape of its own, `hook stripe_paid`, keyed where no client's text is (`statements::own_key`), as `schedule <name>` is ([http.md](http.md#statement-counts)).
- *`/_metrics`*: `fennec_hook_events_total{outcome}` (`taken`, `ignored` by every condition, `replayed`, `conflict`, `refused` by a statement, a binding or the event's own typing, `deferred` by the state -- a quota, a rate, a freeze, a lease, the disk, a standby not holding it in time -- and `unknown`; a verified event of another content type is `refused`) and `fennec_hook_unverified_total{check}` (`header`, `scheme`, `stale`, `size`, `hook`, `signature`: the one place, with the log and the audit line, a 401's check is named); the router's `fennec_router_tenants_hooks_behind`, the tenants a copy of which lacks the directory's hook version; no label names a tenant, a hook, a statement or a collection ([http.md](http.md#metrics)). `monitoring/datadog/` gains monitors on unverified events -- a forger, a sender pointed at a hook not there, or a secret rotated at the sender and not here -- on refused ones and on copies behind, held by `tests/datadog.rs`.
- *The studio* lists a database's hooks in the schema view with `armed`, opens each as `create or replace statement`, and sends a test event for the server's token; its highlighter learns `hook` and `event` (`make studio-highlight`).
- *The SDKs* send nothing new -- a definition is a text -- and their `statements` rows gain the two fields where an SDK types them; `fennec types` writes nothing for a statement no one calls.

### What a hook costs

**A server with no hooks file pays nothing a request, and a database with no hook nothing a write.** The route is one more segment compared where `_call` and `_schema` are, after them; no state is read, no thread started, no collection made until an event is taken; the engine's write path asks a hook nothing, a hook binding no write. A tenant node with no tenant holding a secret keeps an empty map. Held by `a_server_with_no_hook_reads_no_secret` and measured in turns against main, as F3's slices were: through `fennec_http::handle`, a `put`, a `set` and a `del` by id, a `/_call`, each the median of nine rounds over five pairs, a pair run under someone else's load (`uptime`) discarded; `make requests-bench`; `make size-report` for the module and `fennec-server`. A path holding no hook measures as it did, or the slice is not done.

**What a hook costs is held to what it does, and measured in its slice, not here.** An event's own: two HMACs over its body, always, whatever secrets its hook holds (`crypto.rs`'s SHA-256 is scalar, and a hook's `HmacKey`s are made once), GitHub's body digest besides under `key body`, the exact JSON parse, the key's probe, the conditions and the typing before the lock; the runs and the key's row under it; an fsync and the streams after. Measured: verifying a 4 KB and a 64 KB Stripe event in process; a taken event through `handle` against the `/_call` of its statement under an `Idempotency-Key`, the same block's shape; a duplicate answered under the read lock, its block durable already, against the first draft's answer with no wait -- the wait for a durable position is a comparison when nothing is pending; a forged event's 401, both HMACs computed, against a token's, and the same answer for a hook missing, stale or of another scheme, its time held to a valid signature's by the median of its runs; the events a second one node takes durable, against `--sync always`'s writes; under [decision 4](#f4s-decisions-for-the-owner)'s recommendation, a taken event's answer with a pair's standby waited for, against its answer after the fsync alone; `make shard-bench ARGS=latency` with a hook beside its requests. A hook's key row is the file's bytes: its size, and a week of keys at a hundred events a second and thirty days of a `stripe` hook's at ten, given back by compaction (`CompactPolicy`), measured over a day's file.

**An fsync an event costs the buffered writers beside it.** On a server under `--sync 250`, a hook's event is a durable write among buffered ones: its durability holds the sink's disk through its fsync (`Sink::syncing`, [durability.md](durability.md#storage-errors)), and while it does a buffered writer's write behind passes the disk over (`try_lock`), its buffer growing up to `WRITE_HELD`, and a handover due is put off up to twice its bounds -- where it waits the fsync out under the write lock, every request behind it, the stall `Sink::syncing` was made to keep away (a ledger's transfer once waited 52 ms behind a quarter second's `F_FULLFSYNC` on macOS). The syncer meets that four times a second; a hook meets it at its events' rate, events arriving together sharing one fsync (the group commit, [durability.md](durability.md#storage-errors)), and on Linux the sync log keeping each to the log's bytes ([the sync log](durability.md#the-sync-log)). Measured in slice 3, macOS and Linux, each pair in turns and under no one else's load: buffered `put`s' median and p99, and the handovers that waited out an fsync under the lock, beside ten and a hundred events a second, against the same server taking none. A cost past the runs' spread is named on the reference page, where an operator choosing `--sync 250` for its writes reads that its hooks are durable anyway, and why.

### Webhook consumers against other systems

The senders sign alike and the receivers verify in code; what fennecdb adds is the verification, the once-only and the write in one place, with no code of the app's.

| System | Signs, retries | A receiver's work | fennecdb takes | fennecdb leaves |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Stripe (sender) | HMAC-SHA256 over `t.body`, `t` held to a tolerance, "default tolerance of 5 minutes"; "one signature for each secret" while one is rolled; retries "for up to three days"; "doesn't guarantee the delivery of events in the order that they're generated"; "track event IDs to identify duplicate deliveries" ([webhooks](https://docs.stripe.com/webhooks)) | verify in its library, dedupe by `id`, answer 2xx before work | the scheme, two secrets, the tolerance, the key by `id`, retries as the queue, Snapshot events | answering before the work: the 2xx here follows the durable write; thin events, read by a fetch |
| GitHub (sender) | `X-Hub-Signature-256`, the body's HMAC hex, no timestamp ([validating](https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/validating-webhook-deliveries)); answer "within 10 seconds"; no automatic redelivery ([failed deliveries](https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/handling-failed-webhook-deliveries)); "capped at 25 MB" | verify with "a 'constant time' string comparison"; dedupe by `X-GitHub-Delivery` ([best practices](https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/using-webhooks/best-practices-for-using-webhooks)) | the scheme, `ct_eq`, a ceiling per hook, the key by the body or by `X-GitHub-Delivery`, the hook's choice | form-encoded bodies |
| Standard Webhooks, Svix (sender) | `msg_id.timestamp.payload` signed, a list of `v1,<base64>`; `whsec_` base64 secrets; 2xx alone delivered, `410 Gone` disables ([spec](https://github.com/standard-webhooks/standard-webhooks/blob/main/spec/standard-webhooks.md)); Svix retries over about 28 hours and disables after five days failing ([retries](https://docs.svix.com/retries)) | verify, dedupe by `webhook-id` | the scheme, the key by `webhook-id`, never a 410 | `v1a` (Ed25519), a follow-up |
| Convex HTTP actions | TypeScript at a route of the app's, "for receiving webhooks", running mutations; "will not be automatically retried by Convex when errors occur" ([HTTP actions](https://docs.convex.dev/functions/http-actions)) | verify in the function's code, write by a mutation | a route whose work is a transaction | user code; verification as each app writes it |
| Supabase | inbound through an Edge Function, TypeScript ([Stripe webhooks](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/functions/examples/stripe-webhooks)); its Database Webhooks go out, "send real-time data from your database to another system" ([database webhooks](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/database/webhooks)) | the function verifies and writes over the network to the database | -- | a function beside the data, its write apart from its dedupe |
| PocketBase | `routerAdd` and JavaScript reading `e.request.body` ([routing](https://pocketbase.io/docs/js-routing/)) | verify and dedupe in JavaScript | a route of the server's own | a JavaScript engine |

**Also left**, each a proposal of its own once asked for: a generic scheme naming a header and an encoding (`hmac header 'X-Shopify-Hmac-Sha256' base64`), for the senders signing a body alone -- Shopify, Linear -- with the unsigned-id caveat GitHub's has ([decision 5](#f4s-decisions-for-the-owner)); Standard Webhooks' `v1a`, Ed25519, which would need its own arithmetic, held to RFC vectors as `p256.rs` is; a challenge answered from the event (Slack's `url_verification`, Zoom's validation), the one response computed from a body; GitHub's form-encoded deliveries; a cast in a parameter's reading (`$created timestamp from event.created seconds`); Stripe's thin events, whose object a hook would fetch ([decision 13](#f4s-decisions-for-the-owner)); a scoped way for a tenant's own admin to set its secrets, beside the router's admin ([decision 12](#f4s-decisions-for-the-owner)); reloading a hooks file without a restart; outbound webhooks, which `/_changes` and its consumers serve already ([replication.md](replication.md#change-data-capture)).

### F4 in slices

Each a pull request of its own, green and measured, carrying its own docs, as F3's were, and each fails closed: no slice takes an event whose rules a later slice owes. Slices 1 and 2 refuse a hook's definition outside a test; slice 3, the first that takes events, carries every rule of verification, the key, the answers and who may define or call one, so no event is taken a day before its authority holds; a tenant node refuses a hook's definition, and the install of an image holding one, until slice 4 brings secrets and routes there -- a statement defined where no secret can reach it is a hook no sender can use without a word.

1. **The parser and the tree** (fennec-ql, `fennec_core::named`) -- **built** ([below](#what-f4-has-built)). `on hook <name>`, parameters `from event.<path>`, `from event` for a `json` one, and `hook.id`, `hook.at`, `hook.event`, the condition reading `event.` and `hook.event` alone, `event.` in a body a field's path as anywhere; the refusals the text alone answers ([above](#the-form-of-a-hook)); the hook's clauses in the canonical tree, written only where they hold something. Execution refuses a hook's definition in words of its own ("webhook consumers are not built yet"), the module in its own. Owes: `make fennecql-corpus BASE=origin/main`, no tree changed and no stored digest moved; fennec-cli's `kind`, a mark in `Parser::cut` where `where` may follow the hook's name at the text's end, and the shell corpus' lines; the parse time of texts that are no definition, as F1's slice 1 measured it.
2. **The record, the keys and the taking** (fennec-core) -- **built** ([below](#what-f4-has-built)). Op 6 with its tail, `writes_in`, the image, a compact, `Undo::Statements`, a replica's apply, `changes_in`; `depends` and the kind kept across a replacement; `MAX_HOOKED`; `Database::take_event` -- the key looked up and kept in `_hook_events` (rows holding their `until`), the conditions, the typing, the runs in name order, one block -- and a statement broken by its body refusing the events its condition takes, an unread one every event of its hook; whether a key of digits (`event.items."0"`) indexes a list, which no `json` path does today; `tests/statements.fennec` gaining a hook (`FENNEC_WRITE_FIXTURE=1`), which the module opens and keeps through its images. Definitions run where a test lets them, as F3's slice 2 had them. Owes: the module's bytes (`make size-report BASE=origin/main`, all three builds).
3. **Verification and the route on a file's server** (fennec-http's `hooks`, fennec-server). *Prerequisite*: the `Idempotency-Key` replay's fix (branch `idempotent-replay-durable`, [above](#a-replay-waits-for-what-its-first-try-waited-for)) on main, a replay waiting on a durable position; this slice is its second caller. `--hooks`, read and refused at the start as above, `keep` held to more than twice the tolerance and `SKEW`, the tolerance to an hour, `key` to `github`; the three schemes over `crypto.rs`, the scheme read from the headers present, each header once, one `t`, no `.` in a `webhook-id`, two HMACs always, the process's key for a secret that is not there; `POST /_hooks/<name>` judged before `authenticate`, in the order above, every failure the one 401 named in the log, the audit's `refused` line and `fennec_hook_unverified_total` alone, waited by the hooks' own count and never `audit::verified`; the content type after verification; `key body` and `key delivery`; the duplicate under the read lock, answered once its block is as durable and sent as the first's answer was to be; the durable 2xx, and the standby's wait where [decision 4](#f4s-decisions-for-the-owner)'s recommendation is taken; every answer of the table, a sender told as a scoped caller is, never a 410 or a 3xx. With them every rule of [a hook's authority](#a-hooks-authority): a definition by the server's token alone, logged by its shape and naming its hook -- a shape keeping a quoted key, which `fennec_http::statements::shape` holes as a `$n` today, as it holes a string, so `from event.metadata."order-id"` is `from event.metadata.$3` in an audit line and `/_stats/statements` (slice 1's review); `call` by the server's token with an event, keeping no key; a scoped token's call the 404 a missing statement's is, at `/query`, `/batch` and `POST /_call/<name>`; a `call` line naming one logged; no MCP tool; held out of every listing a scoped token reads; the refused events' log lines; the sync's scenario, `NAMED` refusing the definition already. Definitions run for the server's token on a file's server, and are refused on a tenant node until slice 4. The FennecQL reference's section and a page of its own under `site/content/docs/` (Stripe's, GitHub's and a Standard Webhooks sender's setup -- Snapshot payloads, a hook a mode, guards idempotent by the object's id, the addresses' rule at Cloudflare -- the hooks file, `keep` and what a resend after it does, `key`'s two costs, the answers, the one 401 and where its check is read); `make upgrade-check`'s base before op 6, and RELEASING.md's and the runbook's notes, since a primary can write op 6 from here; CLAUDE.md's rules ([below](#what-claudemd-would-gain-from-f4)). Owes: [the measurements above](#what-a-hook-costs), every path holding no hook as on main, and the cost of an fsync an event to buffered writers under `--sync 250`; `make sync-scenarios-check`.
4. **Tenant nodes, the router and the entry** (fennec-http's `tenants`, fennec-shard, `integrations/cloudflare/entry`). Slice 3's refusal lifted; `/t/<t>/_hooks/<name>` asked in `route_tenant` before the tenant is looked up; `<t>.hooks` bound to its file as `.instance` is, put away before every create, install, follow, delete, reclaim and restore, kept by a promotion; `/_admin/tenants/<t>/hooks`, its `PUT` refusing an empty or malformed secret, and `hook-secrets` to the node's admin token alone, its answer in no log or trace; the node's memory of them, a forged event opening no tenant; the router's `/_shard/tenants/<t>/hooks` relay under the tenant's claim, answered once the primary and every standby hold the version or naming the copy behind (`fennec_router_tenants_hooks_behind`), the directory's names and versions, the copy to a standby, to a copy that began to follow, a failed attach tried again, a move's target before the flip and across regions; the router waiting out a hook's 401 by the hooks' count; `replayable` and the entry's keyed rule taking a hook; the router reading a schema declaring a hook with the grammar; `deploy/test/ingress.py` passing `/t/<t>/_hooks/`. The sharding pages' section. Owes: `make shard-bench ARGS=latency` inside its spread.
5. **What watches them** (fennec-http, fennec-abi). The listings' `hook` and `armed` and `/_schema`'s, the slow line, the spans, `/_stats/statements`' shape, `/_metrics`' two families and the router's count of copies behind; the Datadog monitors. README's feature table, the home page and docs index where a visitor picks a database for taking another service's events, the monitoring and sync pages, and the search cache. Owes: `tests/datadog.rs` holding the monitors to the scrapes.
6. **The studio, the SDKs and the shell.** The studio's hooks in its schema view, its test event and the highlighter; each SDK's `statements` row and the SDK lists; the shell's `.help`. Owes: `make studio-test` and each SDK's suite.

**The tests the design asks for**, each failing with its part taken out, beside each slice's own:

- each scheme against its sender's own example -- Stripe's documented header, a GitHub delivery's, the Standard Webhooks reference library's vectors -- verified, and each with one byte of the body, the timestamp or the signature changed refused 401; a `v0` and a `v1a` signature alone refused, never read as `v1`; nine signatures in a header refused;
- the one 401 across scheme, tolerance and ceiling (the review's): a request shaped for another scheme than the hook's, a timestamp past one hook's tolerance and inside another's, a body past one hook's ceiling and inside another's, an unknown tenant, an unknown hook, a hook with no secret and a wrong signature, each answered byte for byte as the others, each computing two HMACs (a count under `#[cfg(test)]`), none opening a tenant (`tenants.get` never reached) or taking a lock, its check named in the log line, the audit line and `fennec_hook_unverified_total` alone;
- headers sent twice (the review's): a `Stripe-Signature` holding two `t`s, each scheme's header sent twice, a `webhook-id` holding a `.`, each the one 401 counted `header`;
- a timestamp a second past the tolerance either way the one 401, counted `stale`; GitHub's, with none, taken;
- `keep` against the tolerance (the review's): a `keep` of twice the tolerance and `SKEW`, a tolerance past an hour or of none, refused at the start and at a node's `PUT`; a copy of an event sent at the tolerance's far edge after its first taking at the near one answered from its key;
- two secrets: an event signed by either taken, by neither refused; an empty secret file, a scheme unknown, a hook named twice and a `key` on a hook not `github`'s, each refused at the start, an empty or malformed secret refused by a node's `PUT`;
- a body a byte past the hook's ceiling the one 401, its read stopped at the ceiling and the HMACs run over what was read; a form-encoded GitHub delivery, verified, 415, and forged, the one 401;
- a verified event clearing no token's refusal count, and a token's verification no hook's; a hostile Stripe account's events, refused, raising no token's wait from Stripe's addresses;
- the same event sent twice at once: one block, the second answered from the key with `Idempotent-Replayed`; sent again after the first's answer: answered under the read lock, no write lock taken; the key with another body: answered as the first, nothing run, `conflict` counted;
- a replay racing the first try's fsync (the review's): the duplicate found under the read lock, and again under the write lock, held until the first try's fsync and streams are done; a crash injected between the landing and the fsync answering neither, the event sent again taken once; the same for `Idempotency-Key` in its own pull request;
- GitHub's key: under `key body` a delivery sent again under another `X-GitHub-Delivery` answered from its key, and two dispatches of one payload one event; under `key delivery` the two dispatches taken twice and a redelivery under its GUID answered from it;
- an event no condition takes: 200, nothing written, no fsync; a hook no statement consumes: 409; a statement broken by its body: 409 for an event its condition takes, an event it would not take taken by the hook's others; an unread one: 409 for every event of its hook;
- `event.type` in a body read as the collection's field `event`, refused at definition where it has none; `$e json from event` keeping the body whole;
- two statements taking one event in name order, the second reading the first's write, in one record with the key; the second's guard refusing: nothing landed, no key, the sender told its `reason` and no name;
- a parameter missing, `null` and of another type -- Stripe's metadata as an `int` -- 400 naming the path, nothing run;
- an event out of order: refused 412 by its guard, taken at a retry once the row it waits for lands;
- the 2xx after the fsync on a server under `--sync 250`: a crash injected between the landing and the fsync answers nothing, and the event sent again is taken once;
- under decision 4's recommendation, a standby's stream held: the 2xx waiting for it, 503 with `Retry-After` past `SENT_WAIT`, the event sent again answered from the key once the standby holds it, or taken once by the standby promoted;
- Stripe's two Event objects for one intent: both taken, the second meeting `stripe_paid`'s guard and writing what is there; an event with `livemode` false on the live hook taken by no statement;
- across a failover, a move and a lease lapsing, each row of the table above, in `tests/lease_nodes.rs`' shape;
- the router resending a hook cut after it was sent whole, answered once; the entry the same;
- a secret set through the router reaching the node and its standby before the `PUT` is answered, a standby down named in `behind` and counted until the repair copies it, a standby attached later, a copy that began to follow, a move's target before the flip and a copy across regions, and never the directory, its archive, a log line or an answer but `hook-secrets`', which a data token is refused; a `PUT` while a move holds the tenant's claim refused 409; a restore answering 401 until the secret is set again, logged as the tenant opens;
- a leftover `<t>.hooks` never arming a tenant of the same name (the review's): left beside a deleted tenant by a failed remove, by a crash between the file's removal and its own, and by a move's source, then the tenant made again under the name and `on hook stripe` defined -- the first owner's events the one 401, the sidecar put away and logged; a promotion keeping the set, a follow dropping it until the router sends it again;
- each place taking none: a replica, a read replica, a following tenant, `--http-read-only`, a marked request, the module, the native library; a sync replica refusing the definition;
- a hook's write waking a binding in its block, and a held `claim` answered by its insert;
- the tenant's quota: 507 with nothing landed and the event taken once room comes; its rate, 429;
- an F3 binary stopping at op 6 in words (`make upgrade-check`);
- `call` of a hook's statement: run unkeyed with an event by the server's token, the 404 to a scoped token, no MCP tool, a `call` line logged, absent from a scoped token's listings;
- `a_server_with_no_hook_reads_no_secret`.

### What F4 has built

**Slice 1, the parser and the tree.** *What it lets a user do: nothing yet, by design* -- every definition is refused where statements run, so no hook is stored that no sender reaches.

*The grammar.* `create [or replace] statement <name> [($p <type> from <source> [= <default>], ...)] on hook <hook> [where <condition>] [comment '<text>'] [check <expr>] [scans] as <body>` reads into `Definition::hook`, a `named::Webhook` boxed as a binding and a schedule are: the hook's name, a word as a statement's is (`valid_name`) and kept as written, a path's segment being read in its case; the condition; and `sources`, what each parameter reads, in the signature's order -- `Source::Event(keys)`, a path into the body, its keys as written joined by dots, as `Value::at_path` reads them (a list of keys was 1.2 KB of the server's code more), none for the body whole, or `Source::HookId`, `HookAt` and `HookEvent`. The parameters stay F1's `Param`s and their sources sit in the hook, so no callable statement's tree or `Debug` moves. `on hook` follows the parentheses directly, or the name where there are none; `where` follows the hook's name directly; `comment`, `check` and `scans` follow in any order. `event` and `hook` are words, read in any case -- `EVENT.type` is `event.type`'s tree -- and a key keeps its case. A key FennecQL writes no name for is quoted as the policy quotes a claim, `event.metadata."order-id"`: the lexer reads a dot right after a name and before a `"` -- "unexpected character `.`" on main, so no text that lexed reads otherwise -- into `Tok::Keys`, the path's names and keys cut by dots, out of its loop and behind `statements` -- the loop's arm for a character no token begins is one call (`unlexed`, `#[cold]`), the refusal and the quoted key's reading (`quoted_keys`) both behind it, since with them inline the loop every text runs was laid out anew, its registers spilled (*Size and time*, below); a key is taken as written, the JSON's own, a character at least, with no `\`, no control character -- a raw newline never reaches a refusal's words -- and no dot, which cuts a path's keys, each refused in one message (`lexer::KEY_HOLDS`). A dot after a name and before a digit, `event.items.0.price`, is the lexer's "unexpected character `.`" still, its words naming the key quoted, `."0"`: a key of digits is the object's key `"0"`, and whether it indexes a list -- `Value::at_path` reads none, as no `json` field's path does today -- is slice 2's to decide with the typing. The token holds a `String`, as `Tok::Ident` does: holding a list of keys, it made every token's copy and drop an arm longer, and the lexer's loop 0.9 KB of the server's code; and it is `Tok`'s last variant, so no token's discriminant moves. A parameter's `from` and a hook's condition read one as they read a name -- `event."id"` is `event.id`, `hook."event"` `hook.event` -- one reading for both (`event_part`); a hook's body refuses it naming where a hook reads one, and everywhere else it is refused as `Parser::quoted` refuses SQL's quoted name -- `o."total"` is "`o.total`, its keys bare, not in double quotes: double quotes make text here" -- and where a name alone stands it is told as "a path with a quoted key", never echoed as the fix. SQL's `o.*`, which the lexer reads as a name's end, names no part of the event: `from event.*` and `where event.* = 1` are refused naming the whole body's form, `$e json from event`. `lexer::Unclosed` learns the key: a `"` right after a name's dot opens one, which its `"` closes and a control character -- a line's end -- closes too, as the lexer refuses it there, so a key left open at a line's end swallows no line after it, as a string would (`the_gate_closes_a_key_at_a_line_s_end`; `the_gate_reads_as_the_lexer` over pieces holding a dot and a control character). In the condition `event.<path>` is an `Expr::Field` named `event.` and its keys, `hook.event` one named so, which slice 2 reads against the event as a `json` field's path is read; in the body `event.x` is the path `x` into a collection's field `event`, as anywhere.

*The refusals the text alone answers*, each at the word it is about and naming the form to write: a hook's parameter with no `from` (`named::HOOKED_PARAMS`); a `from` on a statement taking no hook, refused at the `from` where a word follows the clauses -- where the text ends there, or at `on`, it is left to the next line, which may bring `hook`; `on hook` past another clause, and `on` before any word but `hook`; a `from` naming no part of the event, and `hook.` anything but `id`, `at` and `event`; the body whole to a parameter not `json`, `hook.id` and `hook.event` to one neither `text` nor `json`, and `hook.at` to one neither `timestamp` nor `json` (`named::source_refused`); `from` after a default; a path past `MAX_PATH_KEYS` keys after `event`, a key of no character, and `*` (`named::keys_refused`, held by the tree's refusals, a parameter's at its path and a condition's at its start); a hook's name that is no word; `claims`, `$jwt` and `public` (`named::HOOKED_VALUES`); `every`, `at` or `after` beside `on hook`, in either order, and a second `on hook` (`named::HOOKED_AND_OTHER`); `where` past another clause, and parentheses after the hook's name. In the condition: a bare name, `event` whole, `hook.id` and `hook.at`, a parameter by name or by place (`named::HOOKED_CONDITION_VALUES`), `new.` and `old.`, `slot()`, an inner `get`, an aggregate, `expired()` and `now()` -- it reads the event and literals alone, a function of them (`lower(event.type)`) among them (`named::hook_condition_refused`). In the body: `new.` and `old.`, `slot()`, a quoted key, and beside F1's refusals a `get` without `require`, `returning`, and a body of guards alone (`Definition::hooked_refused`, out of line, which holds a tree made by hand to the same -- a hook beside a binding or a schedule, a name that is no word, claims or `public`, sources more or fewer than the parameters, a path no event holds and `slot()` in a condition among what the parser cannot make, `a_hook_s_tree_made_by_hand_is_held_as_its_text`).

*The canonical tree.* The hook is tag 11 of the definition, after the schedule's 10, a node of kind 7 -- its name (1), its condition (2), its sources (3), each a node of kind 8: the keys under 2, none for the body whole, or `hook.id`, `hook.at` and `hook.event` as 1, 2 and 3 under 1 -- written only where a definition holds one, and `Debug` writes `hook` only there: every other definition's bytes are as they were, `GRAMMAR_EPOCH` stays 2, and the design's `stripe_paid` has its digest pinned (`a_hook_is_in_the_canonical_tree`). `make fennecql-corpus BASE=origin/main` (after the review): 11 013 texts, 22 026 parses, 21 880 read alike; no tree changed and none refused that parsed; 18 parse that were refused, each a hook -- the shell corpus' four, the shell test's, the stored test's, fennec-http's, the module test's two and `tests/hooked.rs`' nine, the design's `stripe_paid` among them; and 55 are refused in other words, each a hook's text or a quoted key that lexes now -- `tests/hooked.rs`' refusals, the lexer's and the shell's tests' keys, the shell test's first line with no hook yet -- or `on` at the text's end past a schedule or a binding, refused in the hook's words now.

*Nothing runs one.* `Database::define_statement` refuses a definition holding a hook before it asks anything else, a schedule's refusal too (`named::HOOKED_NOT_BUILT`, "webhook consumers are not built yet", a query error, 400) -- `/query`, `/batch`, whose block is put back, the shell, the native library and a declared schema's apply all define through it (`a_hook_is_refused_where_statements_run`, fennec-http's `a_hook_is_refused_until_hooks_are_taken`); a scoped token meets the schema change it is (403). The module refuses it as every definition, `UNBUILT`, its parser and lexer built without named statements -- a quoted key there is the lexer's error it was. A definition's record whose text reads as a hook, digest and all, is none this build writes -- op 6 is slice 2's -- and `read_statements` marks it broken (`its text reads as a webhook consumer`, `misread_kind`), never loading it as callable (`a_hook_s_text_in_a_callable_record_is_marked_broken`). `Kind`, `depends`, `MAX_HOOKED` and the kind kept across a replacement are slice 2's.

*The shell.* No arm of fennec-cli's `kind` is new: a hook's definition is `CreateStatement`, a write with one body statement and a schema change with `begin ... end`, as every definition. A mark in `Parser::cut` is new, but at `on` rather than where the plan put it: `create statement pushed on` at the text's end, its `hook` on the next line, stood refused without it, which `a_statement_typed_up_to_a_word_is_left_open` found for four prefixes (`Parser::on_hook` marks it); and `on` at the text's end past a schedule's or a binding's clauses is marked too (`Parser::hook_after`), refused in the hook's words, `named::HOOKED_AND_OTHER`, which `hook` on the next line keeps (`on_at_the_end_after_a_schedule_or_a_binding_is_left_open`). `where` after the hook's name needs none, the form never parsing before `as` and its body, and neither does a `from` with no hook yet where the text ends after the parentheses, refused only where a word follows. Four lines join `a_statement_written_over_lines_reads_as_one`'s corpus -- a quoted key and a parameter's default among them -- and `a_hook_over_lines_reads_as_one_statement` pipes one through the shell, `on` and `hook` on lines of their own, to the engine's refusal.

*Size and time.* The browser module against main (`make size-report BASE=origin/main`, after the review): raw and code the same to the byte with every feature, without the indexes and with none; gzip 0, -2 and +2, brotli +138, +15 and -93, each the report's noise -- the hook's reading, its refusals and its canonical writer are reached from fennec-ql's `statements` path alone, which the module is built without, and its lexer's arm for a character no token begins is main's. `fennec-server`'s code (`make size-report BIN=fennec-server`, this branch and its base, main at #495, each built alone): 4 369.2 KB against 4 348.5, +20.7 KB (+0.48%) -- fennec-ql's +13.7, fennec-core's +5.7, the standard library's +1.2 -- where the first cut was 4 375.0 over the same base, +26.5. The review took 5.8 KB of it: what the parameters read is read again from the parentheses by `hooked_statement` rather than kept in a list every return of `create_statement` dropped (`create_statement` 2.4 KB smaller, 0.3 KB over main's); `Source::Event` one `String`, with no split and collect of keys nor a list's drop glue; one reading of the event's names (`event_part`, 0.8 KB) for `hook_source` and `hooked_field`, 3.1 KB smaller between them; `keys_written` dropped (1.4); and the quoted key's refusals one message. What it added took some back: `*`, an empty key and `MAX_PATH_KEYS` held by the tree (`keys_refused`, 0.5), `Canon::webhook` splitting a path's keys as it writes them (+0.45), `slot()` in a condition, `on` past a schedule (`hook_after`, 0.2). Every one is reached from a definition alone, out of line.

What a text that is no definition pays, over the 10 395 texts of the corpus that are none, counted around the parse loop alone (`proc_pid_rusage`'s `ri_instructions`): under the system's malloc, main 18 254 instructions a text, the first cut 18 346 and this 18 167, medians of 15 runs which spread ±0.5% with the malloc's per-CPU magazines; under an allocator of its own, single-threaded, the same to a tenth from run to run, main 8 799.4, the first cut 8 708.3 (-1.0%) and this 8 680.0 (-1.4%). Fewer, not more: `Tok`'s `PartialEq` goes through a table with its variant more, and the lexer and the parser run what they ran. What moved was the lexer's loop's layout: with the quoted key's call and the refusal inline in its arm for a character no token begins, the loop every text runs (`lex_with`, inlined into `tokenize_vectors` and `parse_exact`) was laid out anew, 264 and 164 bytes larger, its registers spilled, and the first cut's parse time, main's build and the branch's in turns, +0.97% at the median of 7 pairs; with the arm one cold call (`unlexed`, its refusal and `quoted_keys` behind it) the two are 12 and 144 bytes smaller than main's, and `Tok::Keys`, `Tok`'s last variant, moves no other token's discriminant. Its parse time, main's build, the first cut's and this one's in turns, 7 sets of 100 rounds at a load of 1.2 to 1.9: this 1 315.8 ns a text against main's 1 341.1, -1.6% at the median set and faster in 6 of 7, where the first cut was +0.1% in the same sets; with every function of both builds aligned to 64 bytes (`-C llvm-args=-align-all-functions=6`), this +0.3% at the median set, from -0.4% to +1.7% -- where the code falls moves the time by as much as the change does, and this build's lexer falls no worse than main's. A run of the pairs on an idle machine may yet sharpen it.

**Slice 2, the record, the keys and the taking.** *What it lets a user do: nothing yet, by design* -- as F3's slice 2 did, the record lands, and events are taken, while every way in still refuses a definition (`named::HOOKED_NOT_BUILT`, slice 1's words), `Database::allow_hooks` (`#[doc(hidden)]`) letting a test define one: no server writes op 6 before one takes its events, and the upgrade's order is held by a fixture rather than owed by a primary.

*The record.* Op 6 is `[13][0][length][6][history][change][epoch][digest][name][depends][text][hook]`: op 1's fields and the text, then the hook's name, a string, the record's tail. A server reads it into `Stored::hook` (the `statements` feature's) and refuses there, as `a statement record this version does not read`, a hook's name that is no word -- a later version's, never taken for a hook no sender names -- and a byte past it; a tail of nothing is a record cut short (`a_hook_s_record_this_version_does_not_read_is_refused`). F3's reader refuses op 6 as it refuses every op it does not read, so its test of an op past the last one read names op 7 now.

*The module keeps it as bytes.* A build taking no event reads none of a hook: op 6's tail is kept where op 4's is, `Timing`, the bytes as they came behind the op they came in, and written back so -- one field for both, so no statement's clone or drop grew, and nothing in the module tells the two apart but the op it writes back, since it runs neither. A mark (op 5) naming a hook's definition, which no server writes, would be kept by the module as a schedule's is and passed over by the server that next opens the file, as one naming a definition replaced is. The module against main (`make size-report BASE=origin/main`): raw and code -14 bytes with every feature, without the indexes and with none; gzip -4, 0 and +14, brotli -90, -20 and -38, each the report's noise -- `take_statement` 80 bytes smaller, the op kept in the tail rather than branched on, and `image_into` 66 larger, reading it back. The rest is the `statements` feature's, which the module is built without: `take_event`, the event's reader, the key's collection and every check of a hook's record. The docs' figures for the module do not move.

*One write each, and the kind.* `writes_in` counts op 6 as every kind-13 record; an image writes it, a compact keeps it, `Undo::Statements` puts it back with its block, a replica applies it and runs nothing, and `changes_in` hands it over as `ChangeKind::Statement`, which `/_changes` passes over -- each through `Stored::encode` and `decode`, none with a path of its own (`an_image_a_compact_a_replica_and_a_reader_of_the_changes_keep_a_hook`, `a_block_put_back_puts_back_a_hook_s_definition`). The kind is the record's, `named::Kind::Hooked`: a callable, bound or scheduled statement replaced by a hook's, or the other way, is refused naming the `drop` and `create` it needs, while its hook, condition, parameters and body change freely (`breaks_contract` asks it nothing more). `misread_kind` holds a text to its record: one reading as another hook's ("takes another hook's events than its record") or as another kind is marked broken, its hook known from the record all the same, and an op-1 record reading as a hook stays broken as slice 1 had it (`a_hook_s_record_reading_otherwise_is_marked_broken`). `MAX_HOOKED`, 16 a hook, is refused past it, a replacement taking its own place and another hook's statements counting apart. `depends` is its body's: a schema change breaking it is refused at its commit, naming it. A call of one is refused by `callee` ("statement `stripe_paid` takes the events of hook `stripe`: its events run it, and no call does"), `Stored::called` is false -- a scoped token's 404, no MCP tool -- and a `call` or `wake` line naming one is logged as the server starts (`a_call_line_naming_a_scheduled_or_a_hook_s_statement_is_logged`); slice 3 brings the server token's call with an event.

*`Database::take_event`* (`engine/hooked.rs`, the `statements` feature's) takes an `Event` -- the hook, the key, the body parsed exactly, a digest of the body's bytes, `hook.at`, `hook.event` and the hook's `keep` -- and answers what it did (`Took`):

1. *The key*: 1 to 255 bytes with no control character, the body an object, a `keep` past nothing, or 400; kept as `<hook>`, `U+001F` and the key.
2. *Held*: looked up in `_hook_events` -- by `Database::event_kept` over `&Database`, the read lock's, and again as `take_event` starts under the write lock -- and where a live row holds it, `Took::Held(HeldKey { taken, same })`: the statements the first taking ran and whether this body's digest is the first's. Nothing runs, a second body under the key included (decision 7); the caller answers as the first was, and slice 3 makes the answer wait for the first try's durability.
3. *Judged* (`judged`, over `&Database`, so slice 3 may judge before the lock and hold the table by `Arc::ptr_eq`): no statement taking the hook is 409 ("no statement takes hook `github`"); one held unread -- no tree here: a text a later grammar reads otherwise, or no reader installed -- 409 for every event of its hook, its condition unknown; then in name order each condition, read against the event (`event.<path>` and `hook.event`, compared as a `json` field's value is), and for each taking statement: broken by its body, 409 naming the hook and why, its hook's other events taken by its others; each parameter read by its source -- a path by `named::event_at`, `event` the body whole, `hook.id` the key, `hook.at` and `hook.event` -- a value missing or `null` its default, else 400 naming the parameter, its type and what it reads, and held to its type as a document's field of that type is (`Value::coerce`: no cast, a text of digits no `int`), else 400 naming what was found; `check` false, 400. No statement taking it: `Took::Passed`, nothing written, no key kept and no collection made.
4. *Run*: in the block open -- its caller's to land or put back -- or one of its own, landed whole or put back (`may_start` first, the lease asked as it lands): `now()` fixed to the taking for every statement and binding (`Clocked`, F3's), one `--call-max-rows` for all of them (`bounded` once), each body statement through `run_one` -- waking the bindings its writes reach, seeing the writes before it -- then the key's row last: `_hook_events` made by the first event, `{key, digest, taken, until}`. Any refusal -- a guard, a clash, a binding's, the rows -- refuses the event whole, no key kept, and its sender's retry takes it once its guard is met (`a_refused_event_keeps_no_key_and_is_taken_at_a_retry`). `Took::Ran(n)`, the statements that took it.

*The key's row.* `_hook_events` is `key text @hash, digest int, taken int, until timestamp @ttl(1ms)`: each row its own expiry, `until` the taking and the hook's `keep`, out of every read a millisecond past it and swept as every `@ttl` row is -- a hook's `keep` is its own where `@ttl`'s duration is a collection's (`a_key_is_kept_until_its_own_until`: a `github` key of seven days let go and taken again while a `stripe` key of thirty is held, then that one too). `@hash`, not `@unique`: a key past its `until` and not yet swept would clash with the same key taken again. It is the server's own collection by its `_` -- held out of the listings, `/_schema`, a `*` rule, a body, MCP and the tenant's rows (`Database::app_writes`) as `_idempotency` is -- and by its name out of `/_changes` (`cdc.rs`, `HOOK_EVENTS`), the event's writes told (`an_event_s_key_is_passed_over_and_its_writes_told`). A build without the ordered index cannot make it, and so takes no event: a server is built with every index.

*A key of digits indexes a list* -- the decision slice 1 left. `named::event_at` reads a key as an object's member by its name, as `Value::at_path` does, and as a list's element by its place where the key is one RFC 6901 (JSON Pointer) writes: `0`, or digits with no leading zero, counted from the first -- `event.data.object.lines.data."0".price.id` is the first line's price. A JSON value is an object or a list, never both, so a key of digits names one thing wherever it stands: `"0"` the member `0` of an object and the first element of a list, as PostgreSQL's `#>` reads `'{a,0}'`. `01`, `-1` and a place past the end read nothing, as a missing member does, a parameter's default or its refusal following (`a_key_of_digits_indexes_a_list_and_names_a_member`). The body is parsed exactly, so a list of numbers is a list and only `List` is indexed. Rejected: *no list read at all*, which leaves Stripe's line items, GitHub's commits and every array of an event out of a parameter's reach but as the body whole; and *the rule taught to `Value::at_path` and `codec::decode_path_into` for every `json` field*: no FennecQL text writes a field's key of digits -- the lexer refuses `.0`, and a quoted key is a hook's alone -- so it would change nothing a user can write, at a cost in the module's code for a path it never reads. A field's key of digits, once one can be written, takes this rule in both readers.

*The fixture.* `tests/statements.fennec` gains `orders`, an order `o-1`, `stripe_paid` -- the design's example, the hook `stripe` -- and an event it took, the order's `set`, `_hook_events` made and the key in one block (`FENNEC_WRITE_FIXTURE=1`, `hooked::with_hook`), the records before them as they were byte for byte: every build reads them, this one answers the key held at the clock it was kept by, and the module opens the file, keeps the hook's record whole -- op 6 and its tail -- through its images, reads the event's write and its key as rows, the key gone a millisecond past its `until`, and refuses `call stripe_paid` as it refuses every call (`web/fennec.test.js`).

*The upgrade.* `make upgrade-check` gains `OP6_BASE`, `effc12ce`, the last main before op 6: its server refuses to open the fixture in words ("a statement record this version does not read"), an old replica of this tree's server serving it refuses the image and connects again, an old archiver keeps the image it is sent and takes none of its own once a write follows, and its `fennec types` refuses the file; this tree's server opens it, answers its reads, takes its writes and refuses `call stripe_paid`. No primary defines one until slice 3, which brings RELEASING.md's note.

*Size of the server.* `fennec-server`'s code (`make size-report BIN=fennec-server`, this branch and slice 1's head, each built alone): 4 371.8 KB against 4 369.2, +2.6 KB (+0.06%) -- fennec-core's +1.5, fennec-http's +0.7, the standard library's and `alloc`'s +0.4: op 6 read and written, the kind's arms, a call's refusal and the policy's two lines. `take_event` -- its judging, typing, reader and the key's collection -- is none of it: no route of the server calls it yet, so the linker keeps none; slice 3's route brings it, and measures it.

*Time.* A write to a database holding no hook asks nothing new: the engine's write path is as it was, a hook binding no write, and `Database` gains a `bool` beside `scheduling` (`hooking`). Measured as F3's slice 2 was -- `insert`, `set` by id and `del` by id in process, 50 000 each over a fresh database in a file, main's build and this branch's in turns, seven pairs, each run the median of nine rounds and its loop's instructions an op (`proc_pid_rusage`), made once the 1-minute load is under 1.5 and made again where it rose past 2.5: the figures are this pull request's to add once the run is made.

### F4's risks

- *A forged event*, the one thing a hook must never take: a secret outside every copy of the data, the signature checked before anything is read, constant-time, the absent and the wrong one alike. A secret leaked at the sender's side is the sender's to roll, which two secrets at once let a server follow.
- *A replay.* Stripe's and Standard Webhooks' timestamps bound it to the tolerance, inside which the key answers it, a key kept more than twice the tolerance and `SKEW` covering the whole window; GitHub's signature has none, so a delivery captured -- TLS broken, a proxy's log -- is answered from its key under `key body` until `keep` has passed and taken again after, and under `key delivery` taken again at once under another GUID. Its statements are written to do no harm taken twice where they can, which the reference page says beside `key`.
- *An unsigned header trusted*: `hook.event` is GitHub's `X-GitHub-Event`, which its signature does not cover, so a captured `issues` body can be sent as a `push`. Under `key body` a body already taken is answered from its key; one no condition took is not kept, and may be taken by another condition under the forged name, its typed parameters the check -- and under `key delivery` any captured body may. The reference page says a condition reading `hook.event` should also read a field of the body only that event has.
- *A told event lost*: a 2xx answered after its fsync and before a lagging stream sent it, and that standby promoted -- the window every durable write has, closed for hooks where [decision 4](#f4s-decisions-for-the-owner)'s recommendation is taken; a sender's dashboard resends it, which its key lets run once. A duplicate answered before its first try was durable, the review's B1, is no longer one: a replay waits for what its first try waited for.
- *A secret outliving its tenant*: `<t>.hooks` bound to its file and put away before every way a file is made, replaced or removed (B2); left only where `.instance` is left -- a file replaced by hand or by a binary from before, on a file system with no birth time.
- *Statements on one hook coupled*: one statement's guard, typing error -- `metadata.order` missing on a payment link's intent -- or `@unique` clash refuses the event for every statement taking it, their shares held with it in the sender's retries ([decision 8](#f4s-decisions-for-the-owner)).
- *Events refused for days*: a broken statement or a guard that is never met keeps its hook's events in the sender's retries, and past its window (Stripe three days, Svix's disabling after five) they are gone from it. The refused-events monitor and the log line, with each event's key, are how an operator learns in time.
- *A guard taken for a filter*, or the other way: a guard for an event that can never pass retries for three days; a filter for one that should wait takes it as nothing. The reference page's examples write each on purpose.
- *A clock off* by more than the tolerance refuses every signed event: counted as `stale`, its log line naming the skew; NTP is the answer Stripe gives too.
- *The write lock at a spike*: each event one block, bounded by `--call-max-rows`, its durability after the lock; the tenant's rate turns a month's renewals into 429s a sender's backoff spreads out.
- *The keys' growth*: `keep` times the events a second, in the file's bytes until compaction -- thirty days for `stripe`, whose volumes are low; measured.
- *An fsync an event* beside buffered writers under `--sync 250`: their handovers put off and then waiting out the fsync under the lock at the events' rate rather than the syncer's; measured, and named on the reference page where it shows.
- *Secrets on nodes alone*: a restore, or a node's disk lost with its standby's, leaves a tenant's hooks unarmed until set again -- loud, 401 at the sender and a line as the tenant opens -- the price of an archive holding no credential.
- *An older node promoted* stops at op 6 rather than answering a sender 404: the upgrade's order keeps the cluster taking events.
- *CLAUDE.md's room*: the form's rule goes into [crates/fennec-ql/CLAUDE.md](../../crates/fennec-ql/CLAUDE.md), the route's into [crates/fennec-http/CLAUDE.md](../../crates/fennec-http/CLAUDE.md) and the router's into [crates/fennec-shard/CLAUDE.md](../../crates/fennec-shard/CLAUDE.md), each under its 24 000 bytes with room, their titles in the root's index.

### What CLAUDE.md would gain from F4

Slice 2 brought a short rule of its own after the scheduled statements' in `crates/fennec-ql/CLAUDE.md`, as F3's slice 2 did, its title in the root's index: *A webhook consumer's record is kept by every build, and no server takes its events yet* -- op 6 and its tail, kept as bytes by the module; `_hook_events` and `Database::take_event`, the key and the writes one block, a held key answered as the first taking, each row its own `until`; the refusals of a broken and an unread statement; a key of digits indexing a list; every definition refused but a test's. Slice 3 replaces it with:

> **[A webhook consumer takes a verified event once, by its key.](docs/design/named-statements.md#webhook-consumers-f4)** `create statement <n>($p <type> from event.<path>, ...) on hook <h> [where ...] as ...` is kind 13's op 6, its hook's name outside the text, refused by a binary from before. The event's values reach a body through its typed parameters alone (400 where one is missing or mistyped), its condition -- reading the event and literals alone -- picking the events it takes. An event's statements run in name order in one block with its key's row in `_hook_events` (`Database::take_event`), the key looked up again under the write lock; a duplicate is answered from it only once that block is as durable and sent as the first try's answer was to be, a second body under it never run; a statement broken by its body refuses the events its condition takes, an unread one every event of its hook (409). No token but the server's calls one, with an event, keeping no key; a run is the definition's, waking bindings, held to `--call-max-rows` and the tenant's quota and rates.

In `crates/fennec-http/CLAUDE.md`, under *The HTTP server*:

> **[A webhook is verified by its signature before anything of it is read, and every refusal before is one 401.](docs/design/named-statements.md#verifying-an-event)** `POST /_hooks/<name>` -- on a node `/t/<t>/_hooks/<name>`, the signature asked in `route_tenant` in a token's place -- is the one route running a statement without a token: `hooks::verify` reads the scheme from the headers present (each once, one `t`), and checks two HMAC-SHA256s always (`crypto::HmacKey`, `ct_eq`, `v1` alone, the process's key for a secret missing) over the raw body and the timestamp it signs, within the hook's tolerance and ceiling, before the body is parsed, a tenant opened or a lock taken. Every failure before -- headers, scheme, tolerance, ceiling, no such tenant or hook, the signature -- is one 401 after the same work, its check named in the log, the audit line and `fennec_hook_unverified_total` alone; it waits by the hooks' own count, and a verified event never calls `audit::verified`. A secret is a hooks file's (`--hooks`) or a tenant's `<t>.hooks`, never the database's, an archive's, a log's, a trace's or an answer's. An event's key is a value its signature covers, or `X-GitHub-Delivery` where its hook says `key delivery`, kept more than twice the tolerance and `SKEW`. A sender is told 2xx only once its event is durable whatever `--sync` says, or where resending changes nothing, a replay no sooner than its first try; any other answer is the refusal's status, told as a scoped caller is, never a 410 or a 3xx.

And *A token is bound to the tenant it names* gains "a hook's signature, asked in `route_tenant` before the tenant is looked up". In `crates/fennec-shard/CLAUDE.md`, slice 4 adds a rule after *A failed attach is tried again*, the review's B2 in it:

> **[A tenant's hook secrets are its file's, relayed by the router and kept by none of its records.](docs/design/named-statements.md#where-the-secret-lives)** `<t>.hooks` is bound to its file as `.instance` is (`fennec_http::instance`): put away, synced, before every create, install, follow, delete, reclaim and restore, never adopted by another file, kept by a promotion; a follow drops it and the router's version check sends it again. `hook-secrets` answers a set to the node's admin token alone, never logged or traced.
